{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})

Appendix
A
contains some useful properties of the Hessian of
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
. Throughout this section, denote
\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
by
H=H(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
for brevity. The value of
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}
will always be clear from context. We will extensively use the block form of
H
:

H=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\\
H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}.

(51)

Here the diagonal blocks correspond to

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}=2I_{d},\quad\text{and}\quad H_{AA}={\rm diag}\big(H_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}},\dots,H_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\big),

while the non-diagonal ones are given by

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}=H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=-A^{\top},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}=H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}=H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix},

where, for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
,

\displaystyle H_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}=\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}=({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})I_{d}+{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{j}}=-{\bf e}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}.

Throughout the proofs, we will often use a representation of
H
in terms of the Kronecker product. Let us recall that, for any matrices
V\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
and
W\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times s}
, their Kronecker product
U\otimes W
is a matrix of size
pq\times rs
, defined as

\begin{pmatrix}V_{11}W&\dots&V_{1q}W\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
V_{p1}W&\dots&V_{pq}W\end{pmatrix}.

Then we can rewrite
H
in the following form:

H=\begin{pmatrix}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}&-A^{\top}&(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes I_{d}+A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\\
-A&2I_{d}&-I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\\
(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\otimes I_{d}+A\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}&-I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}&I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(52)

The first result indicates that
\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
is positive definite on a set which is sufficiently large for our purposes. In particular, according to Lemma
3.1
, the proper choice of the parameters
\mu
,
\lambda
, and
\rho
yields that both
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}
and
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
belong to this set.

Lemma A.1
.

Let us fix any
\rho\in[0,1/7]
and let
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},{\rm\bf vec}(A))
be any triplet from

\Upsilon(\rho)=\left\{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},{\rm\bf vec}(A)):(1-\rho^{2})\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2},\|\boldsymbol{\eta}-A\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\lambda\right\}.

Then it holds that

\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})=H\succeq\frac{1-2\rho}{4}\,{\mathtt{D}}^{2},

where

{\mathtt{D}}^{2}={\mathtt{D}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})={\rm diag}\left(H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}},H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}},H_{AA}\right)={\rm diag}\left(A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d},2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\right).

We postpone the proof of Lemma
A.1
to Section
A.1
and move to the following auxiliary result. The error-in-operator estimate
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
obviously depends on the nuisance parameter
\chi=(\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
. Let

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}=H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}

stand for the corresponding blocks
H
. Our analysis of bias and variance of the error-in-operator estimate
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
requires examination of
H^{-1}
. According to the block-matrix inversion formula, it holds that

H^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}&-\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\\
-\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}&\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}&-H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\\
-H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}&\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

(53)

We will be particularly interested in

\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}=H/H_{\chi\chi},

which is nothing but the Schur complement of

H_{\chi\chi}
. Before we proceed, we have to introduce additional notation. Let
\mathfrak{m}(\cdot)
be an arbitrary probability measure on the space of triplets
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
. For a function
f(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
(scalar, vector- or matrix-valued), we denote

\left\langle f\right\rangle^{\,\mathfrak{m}}=\int f(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}).

In particular,
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{\,\mathfrak{m}}
stands for

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{\,\mathfrak{m}}=\int H_{\chi\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}).

(54)

The next proposition shows that the inverse of
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{\,\mathfrak{m}}
(and, hence,
H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}
as well) has a nice structure.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
\rho\leqslant 1/2
and let
\mathfrak{m}(\cdot)
be an arbitrary probability measure supported on

\displaystyle\{(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\rho\mu\}.

(55)

Denote the inverse of
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{\,\mathfrak{m}}
defined in (
54
) by
J
. Then the matrix
J
can be represented in the form

\displaystyle J=\begin{pmatrix}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{AA}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(0.5+r_{1})I_{d}&r_{2}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\\
r_{2}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}&I_{d}\otimes R_{3}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{where $\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\mu^{2}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle^{\mathfrak{m}}\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\,\mathfrak{m}}$,}

and the non-negative numbers
r_{1},r_{2}
and the matrix
R_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfy the inequalities

0\leqslant r_{1}\leqslant\rho^{2},\quad 0\leqslant r_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{\mu^{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad\|R_{3}\|\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu^{2}}.

In addition, there exists
r_{2}^{\prime}\in[0,\rho^{2}/\mu^{2}]
,
r_{3}^{\prime}\in[0,\mu^{-4}]
, and
R_{3}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,
\|R_{3}^{\prime}\|\leqslant\mu^{-4}
, such that

r_{2}=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}+r_{2}^{\prime},\quad\text{and}\quad R_{3}=\mu^{-2}I_{d}+R_{3}^{\prime}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle^{\mathfrak{m}}+r_{3}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}.

We move the proof of Proposition
A.2
to Section
A.2
and proceed with a lemma allowing us to control the smoothness of the Hessian of
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let us fix arbitrary
\lambda_{0}>0
and
\mu_{0}>0
and denote

{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{2}={\rm diag}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda_{0}I_{d},2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes\big(\mu_{0}^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}\big)\right).

Let
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
be as defined in (
6
).
Then, for any vectors
{\bf u}
and
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}
of dimension
2d+d^{2}
, it holds that

\left|{\bf u}^{\top}\left(\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon})-\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right){\bf u}\right|\leqslant\frac{4}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}.

The proof of Lemma
A.3
is moved to Section
A.3
.

A.1
Proof of Lemma
A.1

Note that, for any
{\bf u},{\bf v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
{\bf w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
, the quadratic form

\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}^{\top}&{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\\
H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}\\
{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}

is equal to

\displaystyle{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\bf u}+{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\bf v}+{\bf w}^{\top}H_{AA}{\bf w}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\bf v}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}

\displaystyle=\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}.

(56)

Our goal is to show that the right-hand side of (
56
) is not smaller than

\frac{1-2\rho}{4}\left(\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right).

For this purpose, we prove the following lemma in Appendix
A.4
.

Lemma A.4
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
A.1
, it holds that

\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\quad\text{and}\quad\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\sqrt{2}.

The desired lower bound easily follows from Lemma
A.4
. Indeed, using to this lemma and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
2\sqrt{2}ab\leqslant 3a^{2}/4+8b^{2}/3
, we obtain that

2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}\geqslant-2\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{1/2}{\bf u}\|\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{1/2}\|\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|\geqslant-\rho\left(\frac{3}{4}\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{3\lambda}{4}\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{8}{3}\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right)

and

2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}\geqslant-2\|{\bf v}\|\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}\|\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|\geqslant-\rho\left(\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right).

This yields that

\displaystyle\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+{\bf w}^{\top}H_{AA}{\bf w}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(1-\frac{3\rho}{4}\right)\left(\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}\right)+2\left(1-\frac{\rho}{2}\right)\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{8\rho}{3}-\rho\right)\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}.

Taking into account the lower bound

\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}\geqslant\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}-\left(\frac{2}{3}\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\|{\bf v}\|^{2}\right)\geqslant\frac{1}{3}\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|{\bf v}\|^{2},

we conclude that

\displaystyle\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+{\bf w}^{\top}H_{AA}{\bf w}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{3\rho}{4}\right)\left(\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}\right)+2\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\rho}{2}\right)\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{8\rho}{3}-\rho\right)\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}.

Finally, since
\rho\in[0,1/7]
, it holds that

\frac{1}{3}-\frac{3\rho}{4}\geqslant\frac{1}{4}-\left(\frac{3}{4}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\rho=\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\rho}{2},\quad\text{and}\quad 1-\frac{11\rho}{3}\geqslant\frac{10}{21}\geqslant\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\rho}{2},

and we obtain the desired bound:

\displaystyle\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+{\bf w}^{\top}H_{AA}{\bf w}+2{\bf u}^{\top}A^{\top}{\bf v}+2{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1-2\rho}{4}\left(\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right),

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}^{\top}&{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\\
H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}\\
{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1-2\rho}{4}\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}^{\top}&{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&O&O\\
O&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&O\\
O&O&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\bf u}\\
{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}.

The proof is finished.

\square

A.2
Proof of Proposition
A.2

Since the measure
\mathfrak{m}
is clear from the context, we omit it throughout the proof and write
\left\langle H\right\rangle
and
\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle
, instead of
\left\langle H\right\rangle^{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\mathfrak{m}}
for brevity.
Let us note that, according to (
52
), the matrix
H_{\chi\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
admits the following form:

H_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}2I_{d}&I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\\
I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}&I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\end{pmatrix}.

Then, due to the definition of
\left\langle H\right\rangle
it holds that

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}2I_{d}&I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\\
I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle&I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)\end{pmatrix}.

Due to the blockwise inversion formula, we have

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}^{-1}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(2I_{d}-\left(I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\right)\left[I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)\right]^{-1}\left(I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\right)\right)^{-1}.

The latter expression can significantly simplified using the Kronecker product properties:

J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}=\left(2-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}I_{d}.

We are going to show that the remainder

\displaystyle r_{1}=\left(2-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}-\frac{1}{2}

is small and, hence,
J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}
is close to
0.5I_{d}
. Indeed,
taking into account that
\mathfrak{m}
is supported on the set defined in (
55
), we observe that

\left\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\right\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}.

Then, according to the Sherman-Morrison formula,

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}-\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}}{\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}+\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|^{2})}\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|^{2}}\leqslant\rho^{2}.

This yields that

\displaystyle r_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle}{2(2-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle)}\leqslant\frac{\rho^{2}}{4-2\rho^{2}}.

(57)

Since
\rho\leqslant 1/2
, the expression in the right-hand side is at most
2\rho^{2}/7<\rho^{2}
.

We proceed with the block-inversion formula and compute:

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}

\displaystyle=-J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\right)\left[I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\right]=r_{2}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top},

(58)

where

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\mu^{2}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad r_{2}=\mu^{-2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\leqslant\mu^{-2}.

Note that

\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|\leqslant\rho\mu,

(59)

because of
(1-\rho^{2})\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}
.

It remains to consider
J_{AA}
. Since the product
\left\langle H\right\rangle J
equals to the identity matrix, it holds that

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}+\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}J_{AA}=I_{d^{2}}.

This yields that
J_{AA}=\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}^{-1}-\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}^{-1}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}

and, applying (
58
), we obtain that

\displaystyle J_{AA}

\displaystyle=I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}+r_{2}\left[I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)\right]^{-1}(I_{d}\otimes\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle)(I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top})

\displaystyle=I_{d}\otimes R_{3},

where

\displaystyle R_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}+r_{2}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}+\mu^{-4}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}.

Since the norm of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
does not exceed
\rho\mu
(see (
59
)), we have

\|R_{3}\|\leqslant\mu^{-2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\mu^{-4}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|^{2}\leqslant\mu^{-2}+\rho^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\mu^{-2}\leqslant 2\mu^{-2}.

Finally, we study the leading terms of
J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}
and
J_{AA}
. Set
r_{1}^{\prime}=r_{1}/\mu^{2}
. Clearly,
r_{1}^{\prime}
is not greater than
\rho^{2}\mu^{-2}
, and, due to (
58
), it holds that

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}

\displaystyle=r_{2}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=\mu^{-2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=\frac{1}{2}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+r_{1}^{\prime}I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}.

Next, we determine the leading term in the expression for
R_{3}
:

\displaystyle R_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}+\mu^{-4}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)+\mu^{-4}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}+R_{3}^{\prime}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle+r_{3}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top},

where the introduced matrix

R_{3}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)^{-1}

and the coefficient
r_{3}^{\prime}=\mu^{-4}(1/2+r_{1})
satisfy the bounds

\|R_{3}^{\prime}\|=\left\|\mu^{-2}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right)\right\|\leqslant\mu^{-4}\quad\text{and}\quad r_{3}^{\prime}=\mu^{-4}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\leqslant\mu^{-4}.

\square

A.3
Proof of Lemma
A.3

Throughout the proof, we will represent
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
,
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}
, and
{\bf u}
as triplets:

\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},{\rm\bf vec}(A)),\quad\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},{\rm\bf vec}(A^{*})),\quad{\bf u}=({\bf t},\boldsymbol{\nu},{\rm\bf vec}(B)).

Then, calculating the Hessian of
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
(see, for instance, the proof of Lemma
A.1
), we can rewrite the quadratic form
{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}){\bf u}
in the following way:

\displaystyle{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}){\bf u}

\displaystyle=\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf t}\|^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}+\mu\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}A^{*}{\bf t}+2{\bf t}^{\top}(A^{*})^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+2(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}B{\bf t}-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}.

Note that the parameters
\lambda
and
\mu
, appearing in the definition of
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
, may differ from
\lambda_{0}
and
\mu_{0}
. It does not affect the proof, because
\lambda
and
\mu
vanish, once we subtract
{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}){\bf u}
from
{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon}){\bf u}
. Indeed, it holds that

\displaystyle{\bf u}^{\top}\left(\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon})-\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right){\bf u}

\displaystyle=2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}A^{*}{\bf t}+\|A{\bf t}\|^{2}+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}B^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}A{\bf t}

\displaystyle\quad+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}B^{\top}A^{*}{\bf t}+2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad+2(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}+A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}B{\bf t}-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}.

It is straightforward to observe that the difference of
{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon}){\bf u}
and
{\bf u}^{\top}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}){\bf u}
consists of the third order and the fourth order terms (in
{\bf u}
and
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}
):

{\bf u}^{\top}\left(\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon})-\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right){\bf u}={\mathcal{T}}+{\mathcal{Q}},

(60)

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}A^{*}{\bf t}+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}B^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}A{\bf t}+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}B^{\top}A^{*}{\bf t}

\displaystyle\quad+2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+2(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}+A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}B{\bf t}-2\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\top}B\boldsymbol{\theta}

and

{\mathcal{Q}}=\|A{\bf t}\|^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}B\theta+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}A^{\top}B{\bf t}.

It is more convenient to study
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}
separately.
For this reason, we split the rest of the proof into two steps and show that

|{\mathcal{T}}|\leqslant\frac{4}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad|{\mathcal{Q}}|\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}.

Step 1: upper bound on
|{\mathcal{Q}}|
.

We start with a simpler inequality

|{\mathcal{Q}}|\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}

and postpone the proof of the upper bound on the absolute value
{\mathcal{T}}
to the next step. It is enough to show that

\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}\leqslant{\mathcal{Q}}\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}.

The lower bound trivially follows from the inequalities

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}

\displaystyle=\|A{\bf t}+B\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}A^{\top}B{\bf t}\geqslant-2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|\geqslant-\frac{2}{\mu^{2}\lambda}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}.

From now on, we focus on the upper bound on
{\mathcal{Q}}
. It holds that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}

\displaystyle=\|A{\bf t}\|^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+2{\bf t}^{\top}A^{\top}B\theta+2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}A^{\top}B{\bf t}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}+2\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+2\|A\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|.

(61)

The right-hand side can be viewed as a quadratic form of
\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}
and
\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|
:

\displaystyle 2\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}+2\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+2\|A\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}&\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}&\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|\\
\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|&2\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu\|A\|_{\rm F}\\
\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\end{pmatrix}.

(62)

Let us introduce
\varphi\in[0,\pi/2]
, such that

\sin\varphi=\frac{\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}+\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}}},\quad\cos\varphi=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|t\|}{\sqrt{\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}+\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}}}

and consider the matrix

\frac{1}{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}2\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}&\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|\\
\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|&2\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}2\cos^{2}\varphi&\sin\varphi\cos\varphi\\
\sin\varphi\cos\varphi&2\sin^{2}\varphi\end{pmatrix}.

Direct calculations show that both its eigenvalues belong to
[0,2]
. Hence, the operator norm of this matrix does not exceed
2
and we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}&\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}2\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}&\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|\\
\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|&2\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}\\
\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\left\|\begin{pmatrix}2\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}&\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|\\
\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|&2\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}\right)\left\|\begin{pmatrix}2\cos^{2}\varphi&\sin\varphi\cos\varphi\\
\sin\varphi\cos\varphi&2\sin^{2}\varphi\end{pmatrix}\right\|

(63)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}\right).

Taking (
61
), (
62
), and (
63
) into account, we obtain that

{\mathcal{Q}}\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\left(\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|t\|^{2}\right)\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}.

(64)

Step 2: upper bound on
|{\mathcal{T}}|
.

It remains to bound the absolute value of
{\mathcal{T}}
. First, note that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\;|{\mathcal{T}}|}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|

(65)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|.

The right-hand side of (
65
) is quite massive. Let us first examine the terms
\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|
and
\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|
. Due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it holds that

\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

and

\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

(66)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|.

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}

(67)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\sqrt{\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\sqrt{2}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\;\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|.

The inequalities (
66
) and (
67
) help us to simplify the upper bound (
65
) drastically. In particular, they yield that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\;|{\mathcal{T}}|}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\;\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|.

The expression in the right-hand side can be simplified even further. Using the inequalities

\mu_{0}\|A\|_{\rm F}\leqslant\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|,\quad\text{and}\quad\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|,

we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\;|{\mathcal{T}}|}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|A\|_{\rm F}\|{\bf t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\;\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|{\bf t}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\;\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|.

Moreover, introducing

{\bf w}=\left(\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf t}\|^{2}},\sqrt{2}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|,\sqrt{\mu^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\right)^{\top}\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi=\begin{pmatrix}1&\sqrt{2}/4&\sqrt{10}/4\\
\sqrt{2}/4&0&\sqrt{2}/4\\
\sqrt{10}/4&\sqrt{2}/4&1\end{pmatrix},

we observe that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\;|{\mathcal{T}}|}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|{\bf t}\|\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\mu_{0}\|B\|_{\rm F}\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|{\bf t}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|\|B\|_{\rm F}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|+\sqrt{\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda\|{\bf t}\|^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\;\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\;{\bf w}^{\top}\Phi{\bf w}.

Finally, direct calculations show that
\|\Phi\|<2
, and then

\frac{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}\;|{\mathcal{T}}|}{2}\leqslant 2\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\left(\|A^{*}{\bf t}\|^{2}+\lambda_{0}\|{\bf t}\|^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\nu}\|^{2}+\mu_{0}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|B\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}\right)=2\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}.

(68)

Hence, summing up (
60
), (
64
), and (
68
), we obtain that

\left|{\bf u}^{\top}\left(\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})-\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right){\bf u}\right|\leqslant|{\mathcal{T}}|+|{\mathcal{Q}}|\leqslant\frac{4}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\|^{2}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}{\bf u}\|^{2}.

\square

A.4
Proof of Lemma
A.4

The proofs of the upper bounds
\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho
and
\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\sqrt{2}
is based on a simple observation that the operator norm of a matrix
B
does not exceed
\rho
if and only if
B^{\top}B\preceq\rho^{2}I_{d}
. In our case, it is enough to show that

H_{AA}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\preceq\rho^{2}I_{d}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}\preceq 2\rho^{2}I_{d}.

For convenience, we split the derivations into two steps.

Step 1: upper bound on
\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}\|
.

Let us recall that (see (
51
) and (
52
))

H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}=H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\top}=-I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{AA}=I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right).

According to the conditions of the lemma, the triplet
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},{\rm\bf vec}(A))
belongs to the set
\Upsilon(\rho)
.
Then it is straightforward to observe that

H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}=I_{d}\otimes\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\big)\preceq I_{d}\otimes\left(\rho^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}+(1-\rho^{2})\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}I_{d}\right)\preceq I_{d}\otimes\left(\rho^{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}+\rho^{2}\mu^{2}I_{d}\right)=\rho^{2}H_{AA}.

This yields that

\left\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\right\|^{2}=\left\|H_{AA}^{-1/2}H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\rho^{2}.

Step 2: upper bound on
\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|
.
The proof of the upper bound on the operator norm of
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}
relies on the explicit representation of
H_{AA}^{-1}
. According to the Woodbury matrix identity, it holds that

\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)^{-1}=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}

Then

H_{AA}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}\left(I_{d}\otimes\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right),

and we obtain that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes I_{d}+A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right)\Big((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\otimes I_{d}+A\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)+\frac{A^{\top}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{A^{\top}A\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{A^{\top}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{A^{\top}A\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}.

Thus, for any unit vector
{\bf u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\bf u}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{2{\bf u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A{\bf u}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{2{\bf u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A{\bf u}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}({\bf u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta})^{2}+\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}.

By the definition of the set
\Upsilon(\rho)
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}
, and
A
satisfy the inequalities
\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\lambda
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})
. This yields that

{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\bf u}\leqslant 2\rho^{2}\lambda+2\rho^{2}\|A{\bf u}\|^{2}=2\rho^{2}\;{\bf u}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\bf u}.

Hence, it holds that

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}\preceq 2\rho^{2}I_{d}

and then

\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\sqrt{2}.

\square

Appendix B
Properties of the target functional minimizer

In this section, we list auxiliary properties of the triplet
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})
minimizing the objective function
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
. They play a fundamental role in studying the bias of the estimate
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}
defined in (
4
). Our first observation is that
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
belongs to a convex subset of
\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
,
\rho_{0}\in[0,1/7]
(see (
19
)), under mild assumptions. In view of Lemma
A.1
, this means that the functional
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
is strongly convex in a vicinity of
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let us fix an arbitrary
\rho_{0}\in[0,1/7]
and let
\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
be as defined in (
19
). Let

\displaystyle\Theta^{*}

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu}{2}\text{ and }\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{3}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\|A-\Sigma\|\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{3}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\boldsymbol{\eta}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{3}\right\}

and assume that
\Sigma
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
satisfy the inequalities

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu}{7}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{24}.

(69)

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})\in\Theta^{*}\times\mathsf{H}^{*}\times\mathsf{A}^{*}\subseteq\Upsilon(\rho_{0}).

We provide the proof of Lemma
B.1
in Appendix
B.1
below. Its main ingredient is the next result following from the definition of
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
.

Lemma B.2
.

Assume that
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 4\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu/7
. Then we have

(i)

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|_{\rm F}\leqslant\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
and
\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\mu^{-2}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}
;

(ii)

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
;

(iii)

\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
;

(iv)

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}/\mu
.

The proof of Lemma
B.2
is moved to Section
B.2
.
The inequalities
(i)
will often be enough for our purposes. However, sometimes we will need sharper bounds on the operator norm of
A^{*}-\Sigma
and on the Euclidean norm of
A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}
.

Lemma B.3
.

Assume that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu/49
and
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}}.

Lemma B.4
.

Assume that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu/49
and
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
. Then it holds that

\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}}{2}\right\|\leqslant 3\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right),

where
{\bf b}_{\lambda}=-\lambda(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
.

We provide the proofs of Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.4
in Sections
B.3
and
B.4
, respectively.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
B.1

First, let us show that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta^{*}
. Applying Lemma
B.2
(iv)
with
\rho_{0}\leqslant 1/7
in place of
\rho
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

(70)

Next, taking the condition
(ii)
of the lemma into account and using Lemma
B.2
(iv)
, we observe that

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3.

(71)

The inequalities (
70
)
and (
71
) yield that the vector
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
belongs to
\Theta^{*}
. Our next goal is to prove that
A^{*}\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
. Due to Lemma
B.2
(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}{7}\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{3},

(72)

and, hence,
A^{*}\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
. An upper bound on
\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
easily follows from (
71
) and (
72
):

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\theta^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{3},

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta^{*}
and
A\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
. Thus,
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\in\mathsf{H}^{*}
. It only remains to prove that the product
\Theta^{*}\times\mathsf{A}^{*}\times\mathsf{H}^{*}
is a subset of
\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
. For this purpose, let us fix an arbitrary
(\boldsymbol{\theta},A,\boldsymbol{\eta})\in\Theta^{*}\times\mathsf{A}^{*}\times\mathsf{H}^{*}
. Then, due to the definition of
\Theta^{*},\mathsf{A}^{*}
and
\mathsf{H}^{*}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\leqslant\|A-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}.

This implies that the triplet
(\boldsymbol{\theta},A,\boldsymbol{\eta})
belongs to
\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
as well. Hence,
\Theta^{*}\times\mathsf{A}^{*}\times\mathsf{H}^{*}\subseteq\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
, and the proof is finished.

\square

B.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

Let us remind the reader that

{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})={\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)=\frac{1}{2}\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\eta}-A\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\|\Sigma-A\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}.

Since
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})
minimizes
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
, we have

{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},A^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ},\Sigma,\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})=\frac{\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{2}

and then

\displaystyle\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,\quad\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|_{\rm F}\leqslant\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,\quad\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

(73)

This yields the statements
(ii)
and
(iii)
of the lemma and the first part of
(i)
and
(iv)
. In contrary, the derivation of the inequalities

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}/\mu^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\left\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|+3\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|/\mu

is not straightforward. For the ease of exposure, we split the rest of the proof into several steps.

Step 1. An intermediate bound on
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
.
To bound the norm of
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
, we consider the gradient of
{\mathcal{L}}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})={\bf 0}
and

\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)=A^{\top}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})+\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta},

we have

-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ},\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ},\Sigma)=(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})+\lambda(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})

Let us note that
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})/2
. This yields that

\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})+\lambda(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})=-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

or, equivalently,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}(A^{*}-\Sigma)^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

(74)

Thus, due to the triangle inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*}-\Sigma)^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)\right\|+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda}.

Let us introduce
\tilde{\rho}=\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|/\mu\leqslant 1/7
. This, together with the bound (
73
), implies that

\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda}\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda}\cdot\frac{\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{2\mu^{2}}\leqslant\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}.

Hence, it holds that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|\leqslant\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)\right\|+\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}.

(75)

Step 2. Final bound on
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
.
In what follows, we are going to show that

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)\right\|\leqslant\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\frac{35\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{12}.

(76)

Then the inequalities (
75
) and (
76
) will yield the desired bound on
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
. Let us introduce

S=\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad B=\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}S\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}.

Note that, according to Lemma
D.4
and (
73
), the operator norm of
B
does not exceed

\|B\|\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}}{\lambda}\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{2}}{\mu}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}=\tilde{\rho}\sqrt{2}+\tilde{\rho}^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}.

(77)

Here we used the inequalities
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|=\tilde{\rho}\mu
and
0\leqslant\tilde{\rho}\leqslant 1/7
holding by the definition of
\tilde{\rho}
and conditions of the lemma. In particular, (
77
) ensures that the matrix
(I_{d}+B)
is invertible and

\left\|(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{1-\|B\|}\leqslant\frac{4}{3}.

Using the relation

(I_{d}+B)^{-1}=(I_{d}+B)^{-1}(I_{d}+B-B)=I_{d}-(I_{d}+B)^{-1}B,

we obtain that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=-{\bf b}_{\lambda}-\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(I_{d}+B)^{-1}B\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

(78)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

The norm of the second term in the right-hand side of (
78
) in not greater than

\displaystyle\left\|\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(I_{d}+B)^{-1}B\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot\frac{\|B\|}{1-\|B\|}\cdot\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}=\frac{4}{3}(\tilde{\rho}\sqrt{2}+\tilde{\rho}^{2})\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\frac{9\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{4},

(79)

while the third one does not exceed

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\|B\|}\cdot\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma\right\|\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

(80)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\|B\|}\cdot\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu}\leqslant\frac{4}{3}\cdot\frac{\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}=\frac{2\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{3}.

The inequalities (
78
), (
79
), and (
80
) imply that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\frac{9\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{4}+\frac{2\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{3}=\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\frac{35\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{12}.

Hence, (
76
) holds and then (
75
) yields

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\frac{35\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{12}+\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}\leqslant\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|=\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}/\mu,

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
\tilde{\rho}\leqslant 1/7
.

Step 3. The bound on
\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|
.
Finally, we are going to prove that
\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}/\mu^{2}
using the derived bound on
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
. Note that
\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
and then

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+3\tilde{\rho}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\frac{3\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{7}\leqslant\frac{3\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}.

(81)

Similarly to the analysis of
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
, our study of
A^{*}-\Sigma
relies on the first-order optimality condition:

O_{d}=\nabla_{A}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})=(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}+\mu^{2}(A^{*}-\Sigma).

Since
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})/2
, it holds that

\mu^{2}(A^{*}-\Sigma)=\frac{1}{2}(\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}.

Thus, we have

\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}\right)(A^{*}-\Sigma)=\frac{1}{2}\Sigma(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}.

Since the smallest eigenvalue of
\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}
is equal to
\mu^{2}
, we obtain that

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\mu^{2}}\leqslant\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{4\mu^{2}}\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}/\mu^{2}.

\square

B.3
Proof of Lemma
B.3

Similarly to the proof of Theorem
2.3
, the analysis of
A^{*}-\Sigma
starts with the Newton-Leibniz formula:

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})=\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}\big(t\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+(1-t)\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}\big)(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})\,{\rm d}t=\left\langle H\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}).

Here we used the fact that
\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})={\bf 0}
and introduced the notation

\left\langle H\right\rangle=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}\big(t\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+(1-t)\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}\big)\,{\rm d}t.

More generally, throughout the proof, for a matrix-valued function
f(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})=f(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
,
\left\langle f\right\rangle
stands for

\displaystyle\left\langle f\right\rangle=\int_{0}^{1}f\big(t\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+(1-t)\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}\big)\,{\rm d}t.

Note that, according to Lemma
B.1
and Lemma
A.1
, the matrix
\left\langle H\right\rangle
is invertible. Then it holds that
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}=-\left\langle H\right\rangle^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})
, and we have to study the last
d^{2}
components of
\left\langle H\right\rangle^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})
. For this purpose, we use the block form representation of the matrix
\left\langle H\right\rangle
and its inverse:

\displaystyle\left\langle H\right\rangle=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix}.

In what follows, we denote
\left\langle H\right\rangle^{-1}
by
F
and, for any
\boldsymbol{\nu},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in\{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},{\bf a}_{1},\dots{\bf a}_{d}\}
,
F_{\boldsymbol{\nu}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
stands for the corresponding block of
F
. Let us note that all the components of
\nabla{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})
, except for the first
d
ones, are equal to zero. Therefore, for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
we may write

\displaystyle{\bf a}_{j}^{*}-\Sigma{\bf e}_{j}

\displaystyle=-\big(\left\langle H\right\rangle^{-1}\,\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})\big)_{{\bf a}_{j}}=-\big(F\,\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})\big)_{{\bf a}_{j}}

\displaystyle=-F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})-F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})-\sum\limits_{k=1}^{d}F_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{k}}\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{{\bf a}_{k}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=-F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ})=-\lambda F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

Let us elaborate on
F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. Denote the nuisance parameter by
\chi=(\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
and let

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix}.

stand for the corresponding block of
\left\langle H\right\rangle
. Introducing
J=\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{-1}
and using the blockwise inversion formula (
53
), we obtain that

F_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}=-\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}=JH_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}.

Thus, for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, we have

F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=-J_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}-\sum\limits_{k=1}^{d}J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{k}}H_{{\bf a}_{k}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}.

According to Proposition
A.2
, it holds that
J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{k}}=O_{d}
for all
j\neq k
. Then the expression for
F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}
simplifies to

F_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=-J_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}-J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}H_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}

Let us recall that in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we showed (see (
26
))

\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}=-\lambda\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

This yields that

{\bf a}_{j}^{*}-\Sigma{\bf e}_{j}=-J_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})-J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}H_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\dots,d\}$}

and, hence,

\displaystyle A^{*}-\Sigma

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left({\bf e}_{j}({\bf a}_{j}^{*})^{\top}-\Sigma{\bf e}_{j}{\bf e}_{j}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\bf e}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})^{\top}\left(\left\{\left\langle({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})\right\rangle I_{d}+\left\langle{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\}R_{3}+r_{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}{\bf e}_{j}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})^{\top}R_{3}-\left\langle A(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle R_{3}-r_{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}.

(82)

Let us consider the first term in the right-hand side of (
82
). For the sake of brevity, we introduce

\displaystyle\rho:=\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}.

Due to Proposition
C.2
, Lemma
B.2
(iv)
and the assumption
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\mu/7
, we have

\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|\leqslant\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\mu/7.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})^{\top}\,R_{3}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\cdot(2\rho\mu)\cdot\|R_{3}\|/7

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}/3+2\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|/\mu,

(83)

where we use
\|R_{3}\|\leqslant 2\mu^{-2}
due to Proposition
A.2
.
Then we bound last two terms of (
82
). We have

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\Sigma(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\right\|+\left\|\left\langle(A-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\|,

\displaystyle\|\left\langle A\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\Sigma(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|+\left\|\left\langle A-\Sigma\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|.

Using Lemma
B.2
(iv)
and the triangle inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\left\{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\rho\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right\}\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\|+\int_{0}^{1}\|(A^{(t)}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t),

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\left\{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\rho\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right\}\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle^{\top}\|+\int_{0}^{1}\|(A^{(t)}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t).

Since
A^{(t)}\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
, we have
\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
. On the other hand, the vectors
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
, and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}
belong to
\Theta^{*}
. Thus, we can bound their norms by
\rho\mu
, as well as the norm of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
due to (
59
), and obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho\mu\|\Sigma\|\left\{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\rho\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right\}+2\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}/3,

\displaystyle\|\left\langle A\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho\mu\|\Sigma\|\left\{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\rho\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right\}+2\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}/3.

It remains to bound the first terms of expressions above. By the assumptions of the lemma, we have

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24.

This yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\right\|\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\rho\mu\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|,

(84)

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle A\right\rangle(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\rho\mu\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|.

(85)

Combining the inequalities (
83
),(
84
),(
85
) with (
82
), we finally get that

\displaystyle\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}/3+\frac{2\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu}+\|R_{3}\|\cdot(\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|)+r_{2}(\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|),

which is at most
4\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+5\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|/\mu
due to Proposition
A.2
.

\square

B.4
Proof of Lemma
B.4

Since
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})/2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\Sigma(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})\right\|.

(86)

Using Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.2
(iv)
we can easily bound the first term in the right-hand side:

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}}.

(87)

To bound the second term in the right-hand side of (
86
), we use the first-order optimality condition
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})={\bf 0}
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
B.2
(see (
74
)), we obtain that

\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})=-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

(88)

Let us introduce

B=\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}.

According to Lemma
D.4
, the operator norm of
B
does not exceed

\|B\|\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}}{2\lambda}.

Due to Lemma
B.3
, we have

\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}.

The conditions of the lemma yield that
\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|/\sqrt{\lambda}
is not greater than
1
. Indeed, since
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|/\mu=\rho/7\leqslant 1/49
and
\mu\sqrt{\lambda}\geqslant 24\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
, it holds that

\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\left(\frac{2}{7}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{24\mu}\leqslant\left(\frac{2}{7}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{24\mu}\leqslant\left(\frac{2}{7}\right)^{2}+\frac{5}{24\cdot 7}<\frac{1}{8}.

This implies that

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}}{2\lambda}\leqslant\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\leqslant\frac{2\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

and then

\|B\|\leqslant\frac{2\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant 2\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{70\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}.

(89)

Using the identity (
88
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\lambda\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\lambda\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
