\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\Bigg\|

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\lambda\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(I_{d}-(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\Bigg\|.

Due to the triangle inequality and

\left\|I_{d}-(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|=\left\|B(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|B\|}{1-\|B\|}\leqslant\frac{4\|B\|}{3},

it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4\lambda\|B\|}{3}\left\|\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|.

An upper bound on the first term in the right-hand side immediately follows from the inequalities

\left\|\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\sqrt{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

while the second one does not exceed

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\left\|A^{*}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\|(A^{*}-\Sigma)\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}A^{*}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}

Thus, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|\leqslant\frac{4\|B\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{2\lambda}}{3}+\left\|(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{2\lambda}}{3}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)+\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\left(1+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{2\lambda}}{3}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)+\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\left(1+\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{8\sqrt{2}}{3}+1+\frac{1}{8\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leqslant 5\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

This and the inequalities (
86
) and (
87
) yield that

\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\leqslant 3\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{35\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

\square

Appendix C
Results from the proof of Theorem
2.3

C.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Since the measure
\mathfrak{m}
is fixed, we omit the superscript
\mathfrak{m}
and simply write
\left\langle H\right\rangle
instead of
\left\langle H\right\rangle^{\,\mathfrak{m}}
. Similarly, for a matrix-valued function
f
, we define

\displaystyle\left\langle f\right\rangle=\int_{0}^{1}f({\bf u}^{(t)})\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t),\quad\text{where}\quad{\bf u}^{(t)}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)},A^{(t)})=(1-t)\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\circ}+t\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}.

(90)

Our analysis heavily relies on the block-form representation of
H(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
:

H=H(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\\
H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}&H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}.

Here, for any
\boldsymbol{\nu}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
from
\{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A\}
, the block
H_{\boldsymbol{\nu}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=H_{\boldsymbol{\nu}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
corresponds to the second partial derivative of
{\mathtt{L}}
with respect to
\boldsymbol{\nu}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
. In particular, the diagonal blocks are equal to

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}=2I_{d},\quad\text{and}\quad H_{AA}={\rm diag}\big(H_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}},\dots,H_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\big),

while the off-diagonal ones are given by

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}=H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=-A^{\top},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}=H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}=H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix},

where, for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
,

\displaystyle H_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}=\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}=({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})I_{d}+{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{j}}=-{\bf e}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}.

Since the proof of Lemma
5.1
is quite cumbersome, we split it into several steps.

Step 1. Decomposing the Schur complement.

Let us recall that we are interested in properties of the Schur complement of
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}
corresponding to the nuisance parameter
\chi=(\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
:

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{AA}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix}.

In what follows, we denote the inverse of
\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}
by
J
:

\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}^{-1}=J=\begin{pmatrix}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{AA}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
J_{{\bf a}_{1}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&J_{{\bf a}_{1}{\bf a}_{d}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
J_{{\bf a}_{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{1}}&\dots&J_{{\bf a}_{d}{\bf a}_{d}}\end{pmatrix}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}J\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle A^{\top}A\right\rangle+\lambda I_{d}-\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{j}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}

(91)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}J_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}.

We simplify the terms in the right-hand side one by one using Proposition
A.2
, which ensures that
J
admits a nice decomposition. First, according to Proposition
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\right\rangle,\quad\text{where}\quad 0\leqslant r_{1}\leqslant\rho^{2}.

Next, using the identity

A=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}{\bf e}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}^{\top},

we observe that the same proposition yields that the third term in the right-hand side of (
91
) is equal to

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}{\bf a}_{j}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=-r_{2}\langle A\rangle^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\bf e}_{j}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\left\langle({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})\right\rangle-r_{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{d}{\bf e}_{j}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-r_{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}-r_{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle,

(92)

where

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\mu^{2}\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle)\right)^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle.

Let us denote the rightmost side of (
92
) by
{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}
. The sum

\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}J_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}

is nothing but the transpose of the left-hand side of (
92
). Hence, it remains simplify the last term in (
91
):

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}}J_{{\bf a}_{j}{\bf a}_{j}}\left\langle H\right\rangle_{{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})\right\rangle^{2}\,R_{3}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle({\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})\right\rangle+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle{\bf a}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}{\bf a}_{j}^{\top}\right\rangle

(93)

\displaystyle=\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|^{2}\,R_{3}+R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle^{\top}A\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle A^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}+\left\langle A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle.

(94)

We denote the latter sum by
{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}}
. Summing up (
91
), (
92
), and (
93
), we obtain the following identity:

\displaystyle(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left[\left(\left\langle A^{\top}A\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\right\rangle+\lambda I_{d}\right)-r_{1}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\right\rangle-{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}-{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}-{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}}\right].

(95)

Step 2. The leading term expansion.

On this step, we focus on the leading term in the right-hand side of (
95
)

(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left(\left\langle A^{\top}A\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\right\rangle+\lambda I_{d}\right)

(96)

leaving analysis of the remainders for future. We are going to show that (
96
)
approximately equals
I_{d}
. To do so, we use the following proposition.

Proposition C.1
.

Assume that

\displaystyle\rho:=\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\leqslant 1/7\quad\text{and}\quad 7\rho\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}.

Then the following holds. For any
s,t\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\|A^{(s)}-\Sigma\|\leqslant 4\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+5\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|/\mu\quad\text{and}\quad\|(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|\leqslant 3\rho\lambda+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}/\mu.

Applying Proposition
C.1
and using the bound
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\leqslant\lambda^{-1}
, we infer

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left(\left\langle A^{\top}A\right\rangle-\Sigma^{2}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}((A^{(t)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2})\right\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(3\rho\lambda+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu}\right)\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\leqslant 3\rho+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\right\rangle-\Sigma^{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\|(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}^{\otimes 2}(s,t)

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\rho+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Substituting these bounds into (
95
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi})-I_{d}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left[(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi})-(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{9\rho}{2}+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{2\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+r_{1}\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left(3\rho+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\cdot r_{1}+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|+\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}}\right\|.

Let us recall that, according to Proposition
A.2
,
r_{1}\leqslant\rho^{2}
. This inequality, together with the bound

\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right\|\leqslant 2,

yields that

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\left\langle H\right\rangle/\left\langle H\right\rangle_{\chi\chi})-I_{d}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 6\rho+\frac{2\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\right\|+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}}\right\|.

(97)

Step 3. Bounding the remainder I.

We start bounding the term
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}\|
, and then proceed with
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\rangle\right\|.

(98)

We bound
r_{2}
by
\mu^{-2}
. Next, we bound the first term of the expression above. We have

\displaystyle r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|\left\langle A\right\rangle\|\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\mu^{2}\lambda}\leqslant\frac{\|\left\langle A\right\rangle\|\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|\,\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\mu^{2}\lambda}.

Since
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)},A^{(t)})\in\Theta^{*}\times\mathsf{H}^{*}\times\mathsf{A}^{*}
, we have
\|\left\langle A\right\rangle\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3
by the definition of
\mathsf{A}^{*}
, and
\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\mu
since
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
by Lemma
B.2
(iv)
. To bound
\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|
, we use the following proposition.

Proposition C.2
.

Assume that

\displaystyle\rho:=\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\leqslant 1\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24.

For any
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|.

It implies

\displaystyle r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\cdot\left\|\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\left(\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right)\cdot(\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|)\cdot\rho\mu}{\mu^{2}\lambda}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\rho^{2}\|\Sigma\|}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}+\rho^{2}/6+\frac{\rho\|\Sigma\|^{2}\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\lambda}+\frac{\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho/14+\rho^{2}/6+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho/6+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}},

(99)

where we used the condition
7\rho\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}
.

Next, we bound
r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\rangle\right\|
. We have

\displaystyle r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu^{-2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}\cdot\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\right\|\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}^{\otimes 2}(s,t)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right\|\cdot\mu^{-2}\int_{0}^{1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}|\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-2}\,\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\|(A^{s})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|\cdot|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}|\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}^{\otimes 2}(s,t).

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and bound
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\rho\mu
from Proposition
A.2
, we have
\mu^{-2}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}|\leqslant\rho\mu^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|
. The latter is at most
\rho^{2}/7
, since
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\max\{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\}\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\mu/7
from Lemma
B.2
(iv)
. Bounding
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\|
by
1
and applying Proposition
C.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle r_{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}\cdot\left(3\rho\lambda+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu}\right)\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}+3\rho^{3}+\rho^{2}/4\leqslant 3\rho,

(100)

where we used
\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
from (
25
).
Substituting bounds (
99
), (
100
) in (
98
), we get

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}\|\leqslant 7\rho/2+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

(101)

Then, we proceed with the term
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant r_{2}\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\|\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\quad+r_{2}\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}A^{\top}\right\rangle\left\langle A\right\rangle^{\top}\|.

The first term can be bounded by (
99
). We bound the second term using the fact that for each
s\in[0,1]
, the quantity
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(s)})^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is scalar. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}A^{\top}\right\rangle\left\langle A\right\rangle\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(s)}\,(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}^{\otimes 2}(s,t)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}/7\int_{0}^{1}\int^{1}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}\right\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}^{\otimes 2}(s,t),

where we used
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(s)}\|\leqslant\max\{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\}\leqslant\rho\mu/7
from Lemma
B.2
(iv)
and
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\rho\mu
from Proposition
A.2
. Then, we bound
\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}\right\|
as before:

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right\|+\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left((A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(3\rho\lambda+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu}\right)=1+3\rho+\frac{3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}},

where we used
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\leqslant\lambda^{-1}
and Proposition
C.1
for the second inequality. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|\leqslant\rho/6+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\rho^{2}/7+3\rho^{3}/7+\frac{3\rho^{2}\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{7\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Bounding
\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
due to (
25
), we derive

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 1}}^{\top}\|\leqslant 7\rho/2+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

(102)

Step 4. Bounding the remainder II.

Next, we analyze
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}\|}
. The first three terms of (
93
), can be bounded in the same way:

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\{\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|^{2}\,R_{3}+R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle^{\top}A\right\rangle+\left\langle A^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\right\}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\left\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\right\|^{2}\cdot\|R_{3}\|+2\|R_{3}\|\int_{0}^{1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\,\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|\,\|A^{(t)}\|\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)\right\}.

Due to Proposition
C.2
, the quantity
\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|
is at most
\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|
, and

\displaystyle\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}\mu^{2}\lambda/4+\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+\|\Sigma\|^{2}\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|^{2}.

Next, the norm
\|R_{3}\|
is at most
2\mu^{-2}
due to Proposition
A.2
and
\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
due to (
25
), therefore, the above is at most

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\rho^{2}\lambda/2+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu}+\left(\rho\sqrt{\lambda}\mu^{-1}+\frac{2\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\|A^{(t)}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)\right\}

Again, we use
A^{(t)}\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
, and bound
\|A^{(t)}\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3
. Since
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
due to Lemma
B.2
(iv)
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\rho\mu/7
. Hence, the above is at most

\displaystyle\rho^{2}/2+\left(\frac{\rho^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\lambda}\right)\left\{\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}/2+\frac{\rho}{7}+\rho^{2}/3+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{7\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}},

where we used the condition
7\rho\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}
. Thus, the bound

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\{\|\left\langle A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\rangle\|^{2}\,R_{3}+R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle^{\top}A\right\rangle+\left\langle A^{\top}\left\langle(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\right\}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}

(103)

holds. Finally, we bound the norm of
(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle
. Applying the triangle inequality, we derive

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma\right\|\,\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)\right)\|R_{3}\|\,\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)\right)\|R_{3}\|\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\|.

As before, we use the definition of
\mathsf{A}^{*}
, to get bound on the norm of
A^{(t)}\in\mathsf{A}^{*}
and
A^{(t)}-\Sigma
, and, as a consequence, obtain

\displaystyle\|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\|\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\|A^{(t)}\|{\rm d}\mathfrak{m}(t)\leqslant\rho\mu\left\{\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}.

Next, we have
\|R_{3}\|\leqslant 2\mu^{-2}
and
\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
, hence, it yields

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\rho^{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma\right\|\left\{\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}{3}\,\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\left\{\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}.

(104)

Let
\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)\geqslant\dots\geqslant\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)
stand for the eigenvalues of
\Sigma
. Then, due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it holds that

\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma\right\|=\max\limits_{1\leqslant j\leqslant d}\left\{\frac{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)}{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{2}/2+\lambda}\right\}\leqslant\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}.

Applying this bound and the inequality
\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\leqslant 1/\lambda
to (
104
), we deduce

\displaystyle\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left\langle A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right\rangle\,R_{3}\,\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}A\right\rangle\right\|\leqslant\frac{3\rho^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left\{\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}\leqslant\frac{3\rho}{7}+\rho^{2}\leqslant\rho.

(105)

As before, we took into account that
7\rho\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}
and
\rho\leqslant 1/2
due to the proposition conditions. Thus, (
103
) and (
105
) imply that

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\,{\mathtt{R}}_{\eqref{eq: Schur complement of the Hessian -- term 2}}\|\leqslant 2\rho+\frac{\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

(106)

Substituting the bounds (
101
), (
102
), and (
106
) into (
97
), we obtain the desired bound:

\left\|(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\left\langle H\right\rangle^{\,\mathfrak{m}}/\left\langle H\right\rangle^{\,\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi\chi})-I_{d}\right\|\leqslant 15\rho+\frac{5\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}.

The proof is finished.

\square

C.2
Proof of Proposition
C.1

Due to the definition of
A^{(t)}
(see (
90
)), we clearly have
\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|=t\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Therefore, according to Lemma
B.3
, it holds that

\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant 4\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\frac{5\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu}.

It only remains to consider
\|(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|
. The triangle yields that

\displaystyle\|(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|

\displaystyle=\|(A^{(s)}-\Sigma)A^{(t)}+\Sigma(A^{(t)}-\Sigma)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A^{(s)}-\Sigma\|\|A^{(t)}\|+\|\Sigma\|\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(\|A^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\|\right).

Since, according to Lemma
B.1
apllied with
\rho_{0}=1/7
,
A^{*}
and
\Sigma
belong to the set
\mathsf{A}^{*}
, we have
\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
and then

\|\Sigma\|+\|A^{*}\|\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|+\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|+\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{3}.

This yields that

\displaystyle\|(A^{(s)})^{\top}A^{(t)}-\Sigma^{2}\|

\displaystyle=\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(\|A^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(4\rho^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}+\frac{5\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu}\right)(2\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3)

\displaystyle\leqslant 8\rho^{2}\|\Sigma\|\sqrt{\lambda}+4\rho^{2}\lambda/3+\frac{10\rho\|\Sigma\|^{2}\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu}+\frac{5\rho\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}}{3\mu}.

Since
7\rho\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}
and
\rho\leqslant 1/7
, the latter is at most
3\rho\lambda+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\lambda}/\mu
.

\square

C.3
Proof of Proposition
C.2

Due to the triangle inequality,
\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|
satisfies

\displaystyle\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A^{(t)}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle=t\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|+t\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+t\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|.

Since
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})/2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|+\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|}{2}\right)+\frac{3}{4}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|

Let us recall that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}
is a convex combination of
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
, and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
in view of Lemma
B.2
(iv)
. Moreover, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|=\rho\mu/7
due to the conditions of the proposition. Applying Lemma
B.2
(iv)
to
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\frac{3\rho\mu\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{14}+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|+3\rho\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

Using the fact that
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
by the assumptions of Lemma
B.1
, we obtain that

3\rho\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/8.

Finally, the chain of the inequalities

\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho\sqrt{\lambda}/7

following from Lemma
B.2
, implies that

\displaystyle\|A^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(t)}\|\leqslant\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|.

\square

Appendix D
Results from the proof of Theorem
2.4

This section contained proof of auxiliary results related to Theorem
2.4
.

D.1
Preliminaries

We start with the following result playing an important role in the proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma D.1
.

Let us fix any
\rho\in[0,1/16]
and introduce a block diagonal matrix

{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{2}={\rm diag}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d},2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes\big(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}\big)\right).

Consider an event
{\mathcal{E}}
such that
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}
belong to a convex set
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\Upsilon(\rho)
and
171\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}

on
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then, on this event, it holds that

\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\|\leqslant 9\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|.

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

Let us denote
r=9\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|
for brevity and show that
\|{\mathtt{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\|\leqslant r
on the event
{\mathcal{E}}
. For this purpose, we fix an arbitrary vector
{\bf u}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d+d^{2}}
of the norm
\|{\bf u}\|=r
and consider a function

f(t)={\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}),\quad t>0.

We are going to prove that
f^{\prime}(t)\neq 0
for any
t>1
such that
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}^{-1}{\bf u}\in{\mathcal{U}}
. This yields the desired bound, because
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}
is a stationary point of
{\mathtt{L}}
.
Our approach is based on the analysis of the first and the second order derivatives:

f^{\prime}(t)=\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u})^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\quad\text{and}\quad f^{\prime\prime}(t)={\bf u}^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}.

According to Taylor’s formula with the integral remainder term, it holds that

f^{\prime}(1)-f^{\prime}(0)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)=\int\limits_{0}^{1}\big(f^{\prime\prime}(s)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)\big){\rm d}s.

(107)

Let us fix any
s\in(0,1)
and consider
\big(f^{\prime\prime}(s)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)\big)
.
Applying Lemma
A.3
with
\lambda_{0}=\lambda
and
\mu_{0}=\mu
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|f^{\prime\prime}(s)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{\bf u}^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\big(\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+ts\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u})-\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\big){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4s\,\|{\bf u}\|^{3}}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2s^{2}\,\|{\bf u}\|^{4}}{\mu^{2}\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{4s\,r^{3}}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2s^{2}\,r^{4}}{\mu^{2}\lambda}.

Substituting this bound into (
107
), we observe that

\left|f^{\prime}(1)-f^{\prime}(0)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leqslant\frac{4r^{3}}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\int\limits_{0}^{1}s{\rm d}s+\frac{2r^{4}}{\mu^{2}\lambda}\int\limits_{0}^{1}s^{2}{\rm d}s=\frac{2r^{3}}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(1+\frac{r}{3\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

Since
r=9\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|
and
171\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
on the event
{\mathcal{E}}
, it holds that

\left|f^{\prime}(1)-f^{\prime}(0)-f^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leqslant\frac{2r^{2}}{19}\left(1+\frac{1}{57}\right)=\frac{r^{2}}{19}\cdot\frac{58}{57}.

Note that Lemma
A.1
yields

f^{\prime\prime}(0)={\bf u}^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\geqslant\frac{(1-2\rho)\|{\bf u}\|^{2}}{4}=\frac{(1-2\rho)r^{2}}{4}.

Taking into account this inequality and the relation

|f^{\prime}(0)|=\left|\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\right|=\left|{\bf\mathfrak{z}}^{\top}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\right|\leqslant\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|\|{\bf u}\|\leqslant\frac{r^{2}}{9},

we obtain that

f^{\prime}(1)\geqslant\frac{(1-2\rho)r^{2}}{4}-\frac{r^{2}}{10}-\frac{2r^{2}}{19}\cdot\frac{58}{57}.

It is straightforward to check that

\frac{2}{19}\cdot\frac{58}{57}=\frac{6\cdot 58}{57^{2}}\leqslant\frac{6\cdot 58}{57^{2}-1}=\frac{6}{56}=\frac{3}{28}.

Thus, it holds that

f^{\prime}(1)=\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{9}-\frac{3}{28}-\frac{\rho}{2}\right)r^{2}=\left(\frac{5}{36}-\frac{3}{28}-\frac{\rho}{2}\right)r^{2}=\left(\frac{2}{63}-\frac{\rho}{2}\right)r^{2}\geqslant\frac{(1-16\rho)r^{2}}{32}\geqslant 0.

Since
f^{\prime\prime}(t)>0
for any
t>0
such that
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\in{\mathcal{U}}
, the Lagrange mean value theorem yields that
f^{\prime}(t)\geqslant f^{\prime}(1)>0
for any
t\geqslant 1
satisfying
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}{\bf u}\in{\mathcal{U}}
.
Hence, we deduce that
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\|\leqslant r
.

∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
6.1

For convenience, we split the proof into several steps.

Step 1: Taylor’s expansion.

Let us remind a reader that
H_{*}=\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})=\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})
.
Due to the definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}
, it holds that
\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}})={\bf 0}
. Using the Newton-Leibniz rule, we obtain that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}})

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(t\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}+(1-t)(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}){\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}){\rm d}t.

Thus, it holds that

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}=-\left(H_{*}+R\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}),

where we introduced

R=\int\limits_{0}^{1}\left[\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)-H_{*}\right]{\rm d}t.

The representation

-(H_{*}+R)^{-1}=-H_{*}^{-1}H_{*}(H_{*}+R)^{-1}=-H_{*}^{-1}+H_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}

yields the equality

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}=-H_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})+H_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}).

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|S\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|SH_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|+\left\|SH_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{0}\right\|\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|.

Let us recall that

{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{2}={\rm diag}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d},2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes\big(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}\big)\right)

and that
\mu_{0}
does not exceed
\mu
by the definition. Then it is straightforward to observe that
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{2}\succeq{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{2}
and
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-2}\preceq{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-2}
.
Taking these inequalities into account, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|S\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|\left(1+\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\right)

(108)

In the rest of the proof, we bound three terms in the right-hand side of (
108
) one by one.

Step 2: upper bound on
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\|
.

We start with an upper bound on the norm of
{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})
. Note that

\displaystyle{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right)-{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}.

Using Taylor’s expansion with the integral remainder term, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|-{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}+{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\int\limits_{0}^{1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\,H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\,{\rm d}t\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int\limits_{0}^{1}{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-H_{*}\right){\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\,{\rm d}t\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|\int\limits_{0}^{1}\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-H_{*}\right){\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\right\|\,{\rm d}t.

Lemma
A.3
implies that

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t\,H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})-H_{*}\right){\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{4t}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|+\frac{2t^{2}}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2},

Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2}\int\limits_{0}^{1}t{\rm d}t+\frac{2}{\mu_{0}^{2}\lambda}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{3}\int\limits_{0}^{1}t^{2}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2}\left(1+\frac{\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|}{3\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

(109)

Step 3: upper bound on
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|
.

We proceed with an upper bound on the norm of
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}
. Representing the matrix of interest as the product of
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}
and
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}
, we obtain that

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\leqslant\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\right\|\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|.

Due to the Jensen inequality, the first term in the right-hand side does not exceed

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int\limits_{0}^{1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\left[\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)-H_{*}\right]{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\,{\rm d}t\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int\limits_{0}^{1}\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\left[\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)-H_{*}\right]{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\right\|\,{\rm d}t.

Next, we can bound the expression under the integral using Lemmata
D.1
,
A.1
, and
A.3
. Indeed, in view of Lemma
A.3
, for any
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\left(\nabla^{2}{\mathtt{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}+t(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)-H_{*}\right){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\left\|t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t){\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|+\frac{2}{\mu^{2}\lambda}\left\|t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t)\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|^{2}.

Lemma
A.1
implies that

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\leqslant\frac{4}{1-2\rho_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\leqslant\frac{4}{1-2\rho_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|.

Combining this bound with the result of
Lemma
D.1
, we get that

\displaystyle\left\|t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t){\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant t\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right\|+(1-t)\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 9t\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|+\frac{4(1-t)}{1-2\rho_{0}}\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 9\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|.

The last inequality follows from the fact that
\rho\in[0,1/16]
.
Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|t\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})+(1-t){\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|^{2}\leqslant t\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\right\|^{2}+(1-t)\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\right\|^{2}\leqslant 81\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}.

Hence, we obtain that

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{36}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\left(1+\frac{9\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|}{2\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\leqslant\frac{36}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\left(1+\frac{1}{36}\right)=\frac{37}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|.

(110)

The inequality (
110
) and Lemma
A.1
immediately yield an upper bound on
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|
. Indeed, the smallest eigenvalue of
{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}(H_{*}+R){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}
is at least

\frac{1-2\rho_{0}}{4}-\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R\,{\mathtt{D}}^{-1}\right\|\geqslant\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}-\frac{37}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\geqslant\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{32}-\frac{3}{32}=\frac{1}{8}.

Thus,

\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|\leqslant 8

and, as a consequence, we have

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}R(H_{*}+R)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\right\|\leqslant 8\cdot\frac{37}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|=\frac{296}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|.

(111)

Step 4: upper bound on
\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|
.

It only remains to bound
\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|
to finish the proof. According to Lemma
A.1
,
4H_{*}\succeq(1-2\rho_{0}){\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{2}
. This yields that
(1-2\rho_{0})H_{*}^{-1}\preceq 4{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-2}
and then

\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|=\|S\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|\leqslant\|S\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\|\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}H_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|\leqslant\frac{4}{1-2\rho_{0}}\|S\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\|.

Let us recall that

S={\rm diag}\big(\Sigma^{1/2},I_{d+1}\otimes O_{d}\big)\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{2}={\rm diag}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d},2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes\big(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{\top}\big)\right).

Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\|S\,{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|=\left\|\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{*}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(\Sigma-A^{*})\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}\leqslant\lambda^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}

and then

\|SH_{*}^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{*}\|\leqslant\frac{4}{1-2\rho_{0}}\left(\lambda^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}\right).

(112)

Step 5: final bound.

Summing up the inequalities (
108
), (
109
), (
111
), and (
112
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|S\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{1-2\rho_{0}}\left(\lambda^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}\right)\cdot\frac{2}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(1+\frac{\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|}{3\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\left(1+\frac{296}{\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\right).

Since, due to the conditions of Lemma
6.1
,
395\|{\mathtt{D}}_{*}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
and
\rho\leqslant 1/16
, the expression in the right-hand side simplifies to

\displaystyle\left\|S\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}-H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\big)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{8\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2}}{(1-1/8)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\lambda^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|}{3\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\left(1+\frac{296}{395}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{16}{\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|^{2}\left(\lambda^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\left\|\Sigma-A^{*}\right\|}{\lambda}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\|{\mathtt{D}}_{0}H_{*}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}\|}{3\mu_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

In the last line, we used the fact that
64/7\cdot(1+296/395)<16
. The proof is finished.

\square

D.3
Proof of Lemma
6.2

The main idea of the proof of Lemma
6.2
is to represent
\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}
as a linear transform of
({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})
and
(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)
and then apply the large deviation inequalities from Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
. Despite the simple idea, the proof is quite technical, so we split it into several steps for convenience.

Step 1.
We start with an observation that the matrix
\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is close to
(A^{\top}A/2+\lambda I_{d})
.

Lemma D.2
.

Assume that
\rho\leqslant 1/5
. Let us fix an arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\in\Upsilon(\rho)
and let
\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}
stand for the Schur complement of
H_{\chi\chi}
. Then it holds that

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}-I_{d}\right\|\leqslant 10\rho^{2}.

The proof of Lemma
D.2
is postponed to Appendix
D.8.1
. Lemma
D.2
yields that the smallest eigenvalue of
(A^{\top}A/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A^{\top}A/2+\lambda I_{d})^{1/2}

is at least
1-11\rho^{2}
. Then we deduce that the operator norm of

\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}

does not exceed
(1-10\rho^{2})^{-1}
and then

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{1-10\rho^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|.

Thus, we can restrict our attention on a bit simpler term

\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}.

Step 2: explicit expression for
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}
.
Let us elaborate on
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}
. The next lemma shows that
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}
can be represented as a sum of linear transforms of
({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})
and
(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)
.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\rho\leqslant 1/2
and let us fix any
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\in\Upsilon(\rho)
. With the introduced notations, it holds that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}{\bf U}+\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})+(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle\quad-\left(r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\big((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta},

where

{\bf U}={\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}-(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}=\frac{1}{n}\,\mathbb{X}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

and the constants
r_{1}\in[0,\rho^{2}]
,
r_{2}=0.5\mu^{-2}+r_{2}^{\prime}
,
r_{2}^{\prime}\in[0,\rho^{2}/\mu^{2}]
are defined in Proposition
A.2
.

The proof of Lemma
D.3
is moved to Appendix
D.8.2
.
The inequalities
0\leqslant r_{1}\leqslant\rho^{2}
and
0\leqslant r_{2}^{\prime}\leqslant\rho^{2}/\mu^{2}
yield that

\left|r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right|\leqslant\rho^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right|\leqslant 2\rho^{2}.

Then, according to Lemma
D.3
and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}{\bf U}\right\|+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\rho^{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right\|+2\rho^{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|

(113)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left|(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right|+\frac{\lambda^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left|(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\|.

Here we used the fact that

\left\|(A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d})^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Since

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\sqrt{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

the inequality (
113
) can be simplified even further:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\|}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}\,\|{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}\|+2\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left|(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right|

(114)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left|(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\right|.

In other words, Lemma
D.3
allows us to reduce the study of a complicated expression for
\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}
to analysis of much more tractable terms. In the rest of the proof, we bound the summands in the right-hand side of (
113
) one by one applying Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
.

Step 3: large deviation bounds.

Let us recall that on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
we have

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Moreover, according to Theorem
2.5
, each of the inequalities (
36
)–(
39
) holds with probability at least
1-\delta/2
. Note that the conditions of Theorem
2.5
are fulfilled,
because
{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)\leqslant n\leqslant 4n
by our assumptions. Similarly, due to Theorem
2.6
, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, it holds that

\left|(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}{\bf U}\right|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

The union bound yields that there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
of probability at least
1-5\delta/2
where the inequalities (
36
)–(
40
) hold simultaneously. On the intersection of the events
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, the right-hand side of (
114
) does not exceed

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\sqrt{2}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\left(2\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\|+\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+4\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\left(2\sigma+3C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\cdot\frac{\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\sigma\right)\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Finally, since
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}\in\Upsilon(\rho)
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\mu\sqrt{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}}\leqslant\frac{\rho}{\mu}.

This yields the desired bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{1-10\rho^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{1-10\rho^{2}}\left(2\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\|+\frac{\sqrt{2}C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{1-10\rho^{2}}\left(2\sigma+3C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{8\rho}{(1-10\rho^{2})\mu}\cdot\frac{\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\sigma\right)\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

\square

D.4
Proof of Lemma
6.3

As before, we will use the blockwise representation of the Hessian of
{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
.
We would like to recall that
\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}=H_{\chi\chi}-H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\chi\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, where

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}=A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d},\quad H_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}2I_{d}&H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}-A^{\top}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\end{pmatrix}.

The key observation in the proof of Lemma
6.3
is that the off-diagonal blocks of
\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}
are small. To be more precise, let us introduce a block-diagonal matrix

{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{2}={\rm diag}\left(2I_{d}-A(A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}A^{\top},H_{AA}\right),

and consider the difference

Q=\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}-{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{2}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{d}&-AH_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}+H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
-H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}A^{\top}+H_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&-H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\end{pmatrix}.

We are going to show that
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|\leqslant(1+\sqrt{2})\rho+2\rho^{2}
. As usual, we split the rest of the proof into several steps for convenience.

Step 1: upper bound on
\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|
.

Since
A(A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}A^{\top}\preceq I_{d}
and
{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{2}\succeq{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA})
,
it is enough to check that

\left\|{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA}^{-1/2})\;Q\;{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA}^{-1/2})\right\|\leqslant(1+\sqrt{2})\rho+2\rho^{2}.

For this purpose, we fix arbitrary
{\bf v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
{\bf w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
and study

\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}Q\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}.

Due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}Q\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=2{\bf v}^{\top}AH_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}+2{\bf v}^{\top}H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf w}+{\bf w}^{\top}H_{A\boldsymbol{\theta}}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}{\bf w}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{\bf v}\|\|AH_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}\|\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\|{\bf v}\|\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|+\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|^{2}\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}.

According to Lemma
A.4
, we have

\|H_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\quad\text{and}\quad\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}H_{AA}^{-1/2}\|\leqslant\rho\sqrt{2}.

This, together with the inequality

\|AH_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1/2}\|=\left\|A(A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d})^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant 1,

yields that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}^{\top}&{\bf w}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}Q\begin{pmatrix}{\bf v}\\
{\bf w}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\rho(1+\sqrt{2})\|{\bf v}\|\,\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|+2\rho^{2}\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho(1+\sqrt{2})\left(\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right)+2\rho^{2}\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant((1+\sqrt{2})\rho+2\rho^{2})\left(\|{\bf v}\|^{2}+\|H_{AA}^{1/2}{\bf w}\|^{2}\right).

Hence, we proved that

\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|\leqslant\left\|{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA}^{-1/2})\;Q\;{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA}^{-1/2})\right\|\leqslant(1+\sqrt{2})\rho+2\rho^{2}.

(115)

Step 2: upper bound on
\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\|
.

With the inequality (
115
) the upper bound on the norm of
\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}
is straightforward. Indeed, it holds that

\displaystyle\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}=\left({\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{2}+Q\right)^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\left(I_{d}+{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\left(I_{d}-\left(I_{d}+{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right){\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}-{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\left(I_{d}+{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}.

Then (
115
) and the triangle inequality imply that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|+\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\left(I_{d}+{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|+\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\right\|\cdot\frac{\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|}{1-\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}Q\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|}\cdot\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|+\frac{((1+\sqrt{2})\rho+2\rho^{2})\sqrt{2}}{1-(1+\sqrt{2})\rho-2\rho^{2}}\cdot\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

Here we used the fact that

{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{2}\succeq{\rm diag}(I_{d},H_{AA})={\rm diag}\big(I_{d},I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\big)\succeq\frac{1}{2}{\rm diag}\big(2I_{d},I_{d}\otimes(\widetilde{\mu}^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\big)\succeq\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}^{2}

which yields
\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\|\leqslant\sqrt{2}
. Since
\rho\leqslant 1/16
, we obtain that

\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|\leqslant\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|+\frac{((1+\sqrt{2})\rho+\rho/8)\sqrt{2}}{1-(1+\sqrt{2})/16-1/128}\,\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|\leqslant\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|+\frac{9\rho}{2}\,\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

It only remains to note that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\,{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-2}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|{\rm diag}\left(I_{d},\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\mu}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}}{\mu^{2}}I_{d^{2}}\right)\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\rm diag}\left(I_{d},\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{\mu}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}}{\mu^{2}}I_{d^{2}}\right)\begin{pmatrix}{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}\\
\mu^{2}\,{\rm\bf vec}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}\right\|^{2}+\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

and, similarly,

\left\|{\mathtt{D}}_{\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|^{2}\leqslant\left\|{\rm diag}\left(I_{d},\frac{1}{\mu}I_{d^{2}}\right)\begin{pmatrix}{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}\\
\mu^{2}\,{\rm\bf vec}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2}=\left\|{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}\right\|^{2}+\mu^{2}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Then

\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|\leqslant\left(1+\frac{9\rho}{2}\right)\left(\|{\bf U}\|+\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)+\left(\sqrt{\widetilde{\mu}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{9\rho\mu}{2}\right)\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right\|_{\rm F}

and, taking into account that

\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|_{\rm F}\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)^{2}+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},\quad\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

and

\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
(see (
31
) and (
32
)), we deduce the desired bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathtt{D}}\breve{H}_{\chi\chi}^{-1}\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 8\left(1+\frac{9\rho}{2}\right)\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+4\left(1+\frac{9\rho}{2}\right)C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+4\left(\sqrt{\widetilde{\mu}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{9\rho\mu}{2}\right)C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)^{2}+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

\square

D.5
Proof of Lemma
6.4

We would like to remind a reader that (see (
34
))

{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}=-\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi},

where, as before,
\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})=H_{*}/H_{\chi\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})

stands for the Schur complement of
H_{\chi\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})
. The proof of Lemma
6.4
consists of two steps. First, using Lemma
D.2
, we show that
{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}
is close to

\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}.

After that, we quantify the difference between

\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}.

Step 1.
Let us introduce

R=\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}-I_{d}

and note that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}+\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}R(I_{d}+R)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|
