\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|R(I_{d}+R)^{-1}\right\|\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

According to Lemma
B.2
(see
(ii)
and
(iv)
), it holds that

\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

This means that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
belongs to
\Upsilon(\rho^{*})
with
\rho^{*}=\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|/\mu\leqslant 1/112
. Then, due to Lemma
D.2
, the operator norm of
R
does not exceed
10(\rho^{*})^{2}
. This yields that

\left\|R(I_{d}+R)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\|R\|\left\|(I_{d}+R)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|R\|}{1-\|R\|}\leqslant\frac{10(\rho^{*})^{2}}{1-10(\rho^{*})^{2}}\leqslant 1.001(\rho^{*})^{2}=\frac{1.001\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}},

and, as a consequence, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}+\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

(116)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1.001\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

We are going to show that, under the conditions of the lemma, the operator norm of

\Sigma^{1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}

is not greater than
\lambda^{-1/4}
. The proof of this fact is based on the following lemma.

Lemma D.4
.

Let us fix arbitrary
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\lambda>0
. Then it holds that

\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(A^{\top}A-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\|A-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A-\Sigma\|^{2}}{2\lambda}.

We postpone the proof of Lemma
D.4
to Appendix
D.8.3
and proceed with the proof of Lemma
6.4
. Taking into account (
7
) and applying Lemma
B.3
, we note that

\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{5\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{64\cdot 18\mu}\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}.

Due to the definition of
{\bf b}_{\lambda}
and the condition
112\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu
, the right-hand side does not exceed

\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\leqslant\frac{1}{64}+\frac{1}{112\cdot 230}<\frac{1}{63}.

Then, due to Lemma
D.4
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left[\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right]\left(\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{2\cdot 63}\right)\leqslant\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\leqslant\frac{1}{43}.

(117)

In the last line, we used the inequalities

\sqrt{2}+1/126<(17/14)^{2},\quad(\sqrt{2}+1/126)/230<1/160,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu}\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\leqslant\frac{1}{112}.

Let us denote the matrix in the left-hand side by
B
:

B=\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}.

(118)

Then it is straightforward to observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}+\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{1/2}\right\|

(119)

\displaystyle=\left\|(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{1-\|B\|}\leqslant\frac{43}{42}.

This inequality and (
116
) immediately imply that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}+\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1.001\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{43}{42}}\,\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

Due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|^{2}=\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}.

(120)

This yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}+\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1.001\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{43}{42}}\cdot(2\lambda)^{-1/4}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

(121)

Hence, we proved that
\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}
is close to
\Sigma^{1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}
. Our next goal is to show that

\Sigma^{1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\quad\text{and}\quad\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

are close as well.

Step 2.

Using (
118
) we rewrite the difference between

-\Sigma^{1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\quad\text{and}\quad-\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

in the following form:

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right]H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left((I_{d}+B)^{-1}-I_{d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}B(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

Since, due to (
117
), the operator norm of
B(I_{d}+B)^{-1}
does not exceed

\left\|B(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|B\|}{1-\|B\|}\leqslant\frac{43}{42}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right),

we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right]H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{43}{42}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

Combining this inequality with (
119
) and (
120
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right]H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

(122)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{43}{42}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\cdot(2\lambda)^{-1/4}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{43}{42}}\,\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

Finally, summing up the inequalities (
121
) and (
122
), we obtain the desired bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf s}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}+\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1.001\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{43}{42}}\cdot(2\lambda)^{-1/4}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{43}{42}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\cdot(2\lambda)^{-1/4}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{43}{42}}\,\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{-1/4}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}\right\|.

It only remains to note that, due to (
47
), the expression in the right-hand side does not exceed

\displaystyle\left(4\sqrt{2}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}}{112^{2}}+\frac{8}{112^{2}}\right)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{14\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)},

and the claim of the lemma follows.

\square

D.6
Proof of Lemma
6.5

The proof of the lemma is based on the expansion of
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}
from Lemma
D.3
.
According to Lemma
B.2
(see
(ii)
and
(iv)
), it holds that

\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

This means that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
belongs to
\Upsilon(\rho^{*})
with
\rho^{*}=\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|/\mu\leqslant 1/112
. Applying Lemma
D.3
with
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}=\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}
and
\rho=\rho^{*}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}{\bf U}-\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})-(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})

\displaystyle=-\left(r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\right)(A^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\left((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\big((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},

where
r_{1}\in[0,(\rho^{*})^{2}]
,
r_{2}=0.5\mu^{-2}+r_{2}^{\prime}
, and
r_{2}^{\prime}\in[0,(\rho^{*})^{2}/\mu^{2}]
are defined in Proposition
A.2
. Let us denote

\displaystyle{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle=-\left(r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\right)(A^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}

and

{\mathtt{Q}}=\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\left((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\big((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}.

With the introduced notations, it holds that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}+0.5\boldsymbol{\tau}={\mathtt{R}}+{\mathtt{Q}}.

(123)

Let us recall that on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}
we have (see (
43
)–(
45
))

|(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})\|\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

|(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})\|\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

and

\left|(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}{\bf U}\right|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma^{1/2}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})\|\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Since
\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
due to Lemma
B.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\left((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\big((A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\|}{\mu^{2}}\left((1+(\rho^{*})^{2}\right)\left(\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1.001\cdot\frac{4\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}{\mu^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{1+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1.001\cdot\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{7\mu^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda\Psi(n,\delta)}.

Let
\lambda_{1}(\Sigma),\dots,\lambda_{d}(\Sigma)
be the eigenvalues of
\Sigma
. Let us fix an arbitrary
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. Applying the Young inequality

ab\leqslant\frac{a^{p}}{p}+\frac{b^{q}}{q}\quad\text{with $a=\sqrt{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)}$, $b=(2\lambda/3)^{3/4}$, $p=4$, and $q=4/3$,}

we obtain that

\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)}}{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{2}/2+\lambda}\leqslant\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{2}\quad\text{for any $j\in\{1,\dots,d\}$,}

and then

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\max\limits_{1\leqslant j\leqslant d}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)}}{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{2}/2+\lambda}\right\}\leqslant\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{2}.

(124)

Then, using the inequality

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\lambda}{3}\right)^{-3/4}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,

we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{Q}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\lambda}{3}\right)^{-3/4}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\cdot 1.001\cdot\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{7\mu^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda\Psi(n,\delta)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{5\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}.

(125)

On the other hand, since
0\leqslant r_{1}\leqslant(\rho^{*})^{2}
and
0\leqslant r_{2}^{\prime}\leqslant(\rho^{*})^{2}/\mu^{2}
, it holds that

\left|r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}}\right|\leqslant(\rho^{*})^{2}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}

and

\left|\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right|\leqslant 2(\rho^{*})^{2}=\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}.

Then

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{R}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|.

Let us consider the operator norm of

\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}.

Due to the triangle inequality, we have

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\right\|+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|\|\Sigma-A^{*}\|.

Let us note that we have already bounded the operator norm of
\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}
(see (
124
)). Similarly, using Young’s inequality

ab\leqslant\frac{a^{p}}{p}+\frac{b^{q}}{q}\quad\text{with $a=\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{3/2}$, $b=(6\lambda)^{1/4}$, $p=4/3$, and $q=4$,}

we observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\right\|=\max\limits_{1\leqslant j\leqslant d}\left\{\frac{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{3/2}}{\lambda_{j}(\Sigma)^{2}/2+\lambda}\right\}\leqslant\frac{3}{2(6\lambda)^{1/4}}.

Thus, it holds that

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\frac{3}{2(6\lambda)^{1/4}}+\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{2}\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|.

Moreover, Lemma
B.3
and the condition (
7
) imply that

\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\leqslant\frac{1}{64}+\frac{1}{112\cdot 230}<\frac{1}{63},

and we obtain that

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\frac{3}{2(6\lambda)^{1/4}}+\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{126}\leqslant\lambda^{1/4}.

(126)

This yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{R}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\|{\bf U}\|+\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+2\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\right).

Recall that, on the intersection of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}
, we have (see (
31
), (
32
), and (
36
))

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},\quad\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

and

\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Taking into account that, due to Lemma
B.2
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
, we finally get that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}{\mathtt{R}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+\frac{3C_{X}\|\Sigma\|}{2}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{7\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}.

(127)

The identity (
123
) and the inequalities (
125
) and (
127
) yield that

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})H_{\chi\chi}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*})\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}+0.5\boldsymbol{\tau}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{5\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}+\frac{4\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{7\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}\leqslant\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{3}}{\mu^{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}.

\square

D.7
Proof of Lemma
6.6

Note that, due to the definitions of
\boldsymbol{\tau}
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}

\displaystyle=(A^{*}-\Sigma){\bf U}+(A^{*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\quad-(A^{*}-\Sigma)^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}-(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(2A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-2\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}).

Then the triangle inequality yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(\|{\bf U}\|+\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}\right\|\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(2A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-2\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda})\right\|.

In view of (
124
) and (
126
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{4}\,\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(\|{\bf U}\|+\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\lambda^{1/4}}\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}}{2}\,\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\left(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|.

According to Theorem
2.5
, there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}_{3}
,
\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}}_{3})\geqslant 1-3\delta/2
such that the following inequalities holds simultaneously on
{\mathcal{E}}_{3}
:

\displaystyle\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

\displaystyle\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

\displaystyle\left\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\left(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

From now on, we restrict our attention on the intersection of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{3}
. Since on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
(see (
32
))

\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(2\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}+C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2C_{X}}{\lambda^{1/4}}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(128)

\displaystyle\quad+2(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

It remains to bound the term in the right-hand side one by one. Applying Lemma
B.3
and taking into account the inequalities (
7
) and
\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
, we obtain that

\displaystyle(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}\,\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|\left(8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}+C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}\cdot\sqrt{\lambda}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\left(2\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}+C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Due to Lemma
B.4
, it holds that

\displaystyle 2(2\lambda/3)^{-3/4}C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\left\|A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{6\cdot(3/2)^{3/4}}{\lambda^{1/4}}C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Finally, we use the following result to bound the second term in the right-hand side of (
128
).

Lemma D.5
.

Assume that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu/49
and
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/24
. Then it holds that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\leqslant\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{70\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

We provide the proof of Lemma
D.5
in Appendix
D.8.4
. It immediately implies that

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{X}}{\lambda^{1/4}}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{7C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{70\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{7C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Hence, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{(2/3)^{3/4}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+\left(1+3+\frac{7}{2\cdot(3/2)^{3/4}}\right)C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{(2/3)^{3/4}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+7C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\right)\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{(2/3)^{3/4}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+2C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\right)\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

It remains to note that

\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{230\mu}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)

and

\left(\frac{\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}+2C_{X}\|\Sigma\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}}{14}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\tau}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(3/2)^{3/4}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2\sqrt{2}\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{12\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{25\lambda^{1/4}}\left(\left(\frac{17\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{160\mu}\right)\sqrt{\Psi(n,\delta)}.

\square

D.8
Auxiliary results

D.8.1
Proof of Lemma
D.2

Applying Proposition
A.2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[-r_{1}+\frac{2r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left(1+r_{3}^{\prime}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\right]A^{\top}A

(129)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}I_{d}+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A+A^{\top}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right),

where

0\leqslant r_{1}\leqslant\rho^{2},\quad 0\leqslant r_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{\mu^{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad 0\leqslant r_{3}^{\prime}\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu^{4}}.

In the rest of the proof, we study the terms in the right-hand side of (
129
) one by one. First, let us note that the inequalities
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\leqslant\rho^{2}(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})
and
\rho^{2}\leqslant 1/2
yield that

\displaystyle\left|-r_{1}+\frac{2r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left(1+r_{3}^{\prime}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}+\frac{2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}(\mu^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}{\mu^{4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}+2\rho^{2}+2\rho^{4}\leqslant 3\rho^{2}+\frac{2\rho^{2}}{25}.

Then the first term in the right-hand side of (
129
) does not exceed

\left(3\rho^{2}+\frac{2\rho^{2}}{25}\right)\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}A^{\top}A\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant 6\rho^{2}+2\rho^{4}\leqslant 6\rho^{2}+\frac{4\rho^{2}}{25}.

Second, it holds that

\frac{\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\rho^{2}\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda}=\rho^{2}.

Finally, let us consider the latter term in the right-hand side of (
129
). The triangle inequality and the submultiplicativity of the operator norm imply that

\displaystyle\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}A+A^{\top}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\|\left\|A^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\cdot\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot\sqrt{2}\cdot\rho\mu\sqrt{\lambda}\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}\rho\mu}{\sqrt{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}}\cdot\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}{\sqrt{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}}\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}\rho\mu}{\mu}\cdot\rho=2\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}.

Hence, it holds that

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\breve{H}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}A+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}-I_{d}\right\|\leqslant 6\rho^{2}+\frac{4\rho^{2}}{25}+\rho^{2}+2\sqrt{2}\rho^{2}\leqslant 10\rho^{2}.

\square

D.8.2
Proof of Lemma
D.3

The proof of Lemma
D.3
relies on the expression (
52
) for the Hessian of
\nabla^{2}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\upsilon})
and Proposition
A.2
.
Let us denote the inverse of
H_{\chi\chi}
by
J
.
It holds that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}J{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\\
J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}&J_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\\
{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{A}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{A}+H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{AA}{\bf\mathfrak{z}}_{A}.

In the rest of the proof, we study the terms in the right-hand-side one by one. Using Proposition
A.2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}

\displaystyle=r_{2}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes I_{d}+A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{2}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+r_{2}A^{\top}\otimes\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{2}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}+r_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)A^{\top},

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}
. The Woodbury matrix identity yields that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\frac{\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\frac{\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}.

Then we have

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{A\boldsymbol{\eta}}\mathfrak{z}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}).

(130)

Similarly, we note that

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\mathfrak{z}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=-A^{\top}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\mathfrak{z}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=-\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}\right)A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}).

(131)

Due to the properties of the Kronecker product and the vectorization operator (see, for instance,
[
Puchkin and Rakhuba, 2024
, Appendix A, eq. (25)]
), it holds that

(I_{d}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\,^{\top}){\rm\bf vec}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)={\rm\bf vec}\left(I_{d}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)=(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}.

Thus, we obtain that

H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\eta}}J_{\boldsymbol{\eta}A}\mathfrak{z}_{A}=-r_{2}\mu^{2}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}.

(132)

It remains to consider
H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{AA}\mathfrak{z}_{A}
.
Let us focus on
J_{AA}
. Note that, due to (
52
), the matrix
H_{\chi\chi}
can be represented in the following compact form using the Kronecker product:

H_{\chi\chi}=\begin{pmatrix}2I_{d}&-I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\\
-I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}&I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})\end{pmatrix}.

Then, according to the block-inversion formula (see (
53
) in Appendix
A
), it holds that

\displaystyle J_{AA}

\displaystyle=\left(I_{d}\otimes(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})-\frac{1}{2}\left(I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right)^{-1}=I_{d}\otimes\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)^{-1}.

Using the Woodbury matrix identity, we obtain that

J_{AA}=I_{d}\otimes\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}(2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right).

Since
\mathfrak{z}_{A}=\mu^{2}{\rm\bf vec}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)
, we have

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{AA}\mathfrak{z}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mu^{2}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes I_{d}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}(2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{2}\left(A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\left(I_{d}\otimes\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}I_{d}-\frac{1}{\mu^{2}(2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

(133)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right).

Using the properties of the Kronecker product and the vectorization operator
{\rm\bf vec}
one more time, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}\otimes\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle={\rm\bf vec}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right)

(134)

\displaystyle=\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})

and

\displaystyle\left(A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\left(I_{d}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left(A^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right){\rm\bf vec}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\rm\bf vec}\left(A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(135)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}.

Substituting the equalities (
134
) and (
135
) into (
133
), we obtain that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}A}J_{AA}\mathfrak{z}_{A}

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\big((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}.

(136)

Summing up (
130
), (
131
), (
132
), and (
136
), we conclude that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}J\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{1}{2}+r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\big((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad+(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}).

Due to the definition of
r_{2}
(see Proposition
A.2
), it holds that
r_{2}=0.5\mu^{-2}+r_{2}^{\prime}
, where
0\leqslant r_{2}^{\prime}\leqslant\rho^{2}/\mu^{2}
. Using this identity, we can simplify the coefficient before
A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}
:

\displaystyle\frac{2\mu^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}-\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}-\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}-\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}.

Finally, taking into account the representation
{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}={\bf U}+(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle H_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\chi}J\mathfrak{z}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}{\bf U}+\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})+(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle\quad-\left(r_{1}-\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})+\frac{r_{2}\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\left((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}{2(\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2})}+\frac{r_{2}^{\prime}\mu^{4}}{\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\right)A^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}}\big((A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}\big)\boldsymbol{\theta}.

\square

D.8.3
Proof of Lemma
D.4

Step 1: a bound on the operator norm.

Let us start with an upper bound on the operator norm of

\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(A^{\top}A-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}.

Due to the definition of
S
and the triangle inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(A^{\top}A-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma(A-\Sigma)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(A^{\top}-\Sigma)(A-\Sigma)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|.

Let us note that

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma\right\|\leqslant\sqrt{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

(137)

Taking into account submultiplicativity of the operator norm, we obtain that

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma(A-\Sigma)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\|A-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}

and

\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}(A^{\top}-\Sigma)(A-\Sigma)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|A-\Sigma\|^{2}}{2\lambda}.

Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(A^{\top}A-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\|A-\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{2}{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A-\Sigma\|^{2}}{2\lambda}.

\square

D.8.4
Proof of Lemma
D.5

The proof relies on the identity

\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})=-\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

following from the first-order optimality condition
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})={\bf 0}
(see (
74
) in the proof of Lemma
B.2
for the details).
Let us introduce

B=\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left((A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}-\Sigma^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}.

Then we can rewrite the difference
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda})
in the following form:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\left(I_{d}-(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}(A^{*}-\Sigma)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}.

The inequalities

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{2}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\quad\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}(A^{*})^{\top}A^{*}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}(A^{*})^{\top}\right\|\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\lambda}},

and

\left\|I_{d}-(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|=\left\|B(I_{d}+B)^{-1}\right\|\leqslant\frac{\|B\|}{1-\|B\|}

yield that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\leqslant\left(\frac{\|B\|}{1-\|B\|}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{2\sqrt{2\lambda}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
B.4
, we deduce that (see (
89
))

\|B\|\leqslant\frac{2\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant 2\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{70\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{4\|B\|}{3}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{2\sqrt{2\lambda}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{8\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{3\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{2\sqrt{2\lambda}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}\cdot\frac{\|A^{*}-\Sigma\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{7\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}\left(\left(\frac{14\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\mu}\right)^{2}+\frac{70\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

Appendix E
Concentration inequalities

E.1
Proof of Theorem
2.5

Following the ideas of
Zhivotovskiy [2024]
, we use the tools of PAC-Bayesian analysis to prove a large deviation inequality. The core of this approach is the following variational inequality (see, e. g.,
[
Catoni and Giulini, 2017
, Proposition 2.1]
).

Lemma E.1
.

Let
{\bf X},{\bf X}_{1},\dots,{\bf X}_{n}
be i.i.d. random elements on a measurable space
{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
\Theta
be a parameter space equipped with a measure
\mu
(which is also referred to as prior). Let
f:{\mathcal{X}}\times\Theta\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\sim\rho}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f({\bf X}_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\sim\rho}\log\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}e^{f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta})}+\frac{{\mathcal{KL}}(\rho,\mu)+\log(1/\delta)}{n}

simultaneously for all
\rho\ll\mu
.

In what follows, we show that a high probability upper bound on
\|B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\|_{\rm F}
can be deduced from the PAC-Bayes variational inequality.
We split the proof into several steps for convenience.

Step 0: auxiliary constants
.
Before we move to the proof, let us introduce auxiliary constants. We define

\beta=2\,{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2})\,{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}),

(138)

R=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}\,{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2})\,{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})},

(139)

and

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{X}\sqrt{\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\,\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}}\|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2})\,{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{4n}},

(140)

where
C_{X}
is defined in Assumption
2.1
.
We move to the proof of Theorem
2.5
.

Step 1: a variational inequality.

On this step, we make some preparations. We specify a prior
\mu
and a family of measures
\rho
from Lemma
E.1
. A reader will immediately see the result of these efforts on the second step where we relate the variational approach with a high probability upper bound on the Frobenius norm of
B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)
.
Let us introduce
\boldsymbol{\xi}=\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}=\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{i}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, and a random matrix

\Psi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}ZA\Sigma^{1/2},

where
Z
is a matrix of size
(q\times d)
with i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries and
\beta
is defined in (
138
). Note that

{\rm\bf vec}(\Psi)\sim{\mathcal{N}}\big({\bf 0},\beta^{-1}\,(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2})\otimes(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})\big)

in this case (see, for instance,
[
Leng and Pan, 2018
]
for the details). Denote its density (with respect to the volume measure on the image of
(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2})\otimes(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})
) by
\mathsf{p}
and, for any
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, define

\mathsf{p}_{U}({\bf x})=\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}\,\mathsf{p}\big({\bf x}-{\rm\bf vec}(U)\big)\mathbbm{1}(\|{\bf x}-{\rm\bf vec}(U)\|\leqslant R),

where
{\rm\bf vec}(U)\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
is a vector obtained by reshaping of
U
and
\pi_{R}\in(0,1)
is a normalizing constant.
Then
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}+\dots\boldsymbol{\xi}_{n}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}_{n})/n-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}
can be represented in the following form:

\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}=\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{X}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}\right).

According to Lemma
E.1
, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}
, such that
\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{E}})\geqslant 1-\delta
and

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{X}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}+\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})+\log(1/\delta)}{n}

simultaneously for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
on
{\mathcal{E}}
. From now on, we restrict our attention on this event.

Step 2: relating the variational inequality with the Frobenius norm.
Rewrite the Frobenius norm of
B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times q},\\
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1\end{subarray}}{\rm Tr}\big(VB(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}\big)=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times q},\\
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}{\bf X}_{i}^{\top}A^{\top}VB{\bf X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}{\bf X}^{\top}A^{\top}VB{\bf X}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}U=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}VB\Sigma^{1/2},\\
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}U\boldsymbol{\xi}\right).

On the event
{\mathcal{E}}
, defined on Step 1, it holds that

\displaystyle\lambda\|B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}U=\Sigma^{1/2}VB\Sigma^{1/2},\\
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1\end{subarray}}\Bigg(-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}}+\frac{{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\Bigg).

(141)

It only remains to bound the terms in the right hand side.

Step 3: a bound on
{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})
.

Applying Lemma
E.2
with
P=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}
,
Q=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}
, and
U=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}VB\Sigma^{1/2}
,
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1
, we obtain that

{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})\leqslant\log 2+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\|(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top})^{\dagger}U(B\Sigma^{1/2})^{\dagger}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant\log 2+\frac{\beta}{2}\|V\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\log 2+\frac{\beta}{2}.

(142)

Step 4: a bound on
\mathbb{E}_{{\rm\bf vec}(\Phi)\sim\mathsf{p}_{U}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}}
.

Note that, since
U=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}VB\Sigma^{1/2}
,
\|V\|_{\rm F}=1
, the following inequality holds almost surely:

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant 2\|U\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2R^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}\|^{2}\|V\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|^{2}+2R^{2}

\displaystyle=2\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|+2R^{2}.

By the definition of
R
(see (
139
) and (
138
)), we have

\|\Phi\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant 4\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|\quad\text{almost surely.}

Note that this inequality yields that
\lambda
, defined in (
140
), does not exceed
1/(C_{X}\|\Phi\|_{\rm F})
.
Hence, due to Assumption
2.1
, it holds that

-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi})+\log\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Phi\boldsymbol{\xi}}\leqslant C_{X}^{2}\lambda^{2}\|\Phi\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant 4C_{X}^{2}\lambda^{2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|.

(143)

Summing up the inequalitites (
141
), (
142
), and (
143
), we obtain that

\lambda\|B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}\|_{\rm F}\leqslant 4C_{X}^{2}\lambda^{2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|+\frac{\beta/2+\log(2/\delta)}{n}

on the event
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Dividing both parts by
\lambda
and taking into account (
138
) and (
140
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|B(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)A^{\top}\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}\|\,\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\top}A\Sigma^{1/2}){\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

with probability at least
1-\delta
.

E.2
Proof of Theorem
2.6

The proof of this statement is also based on the PAC-Bayesian approach. Note that

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}B{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\right\|=\sup\limits_{\|{\bf v}\|=1}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}{\bf v}^{\top}B{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v},\\
\|{\bf v}\|=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}{\bf u}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}.

Let
\mu
be the probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
corresponding to the Gaussian distribution

\mathcal{N}\left({\bf 0},\frac{1}{2{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})}\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\right).

For any
{\bf u}
, such that
{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v}
for some unit vector
{\bf v}
, define a probability measure
\rho_{\bf u}
by the formula

{\rm d}\rho_{\bf u}({\bf x})=\frac{1}{\pi}\mathbbm{1}\left(\|{\bf x}-{\bf u}\|\leqslant\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|\right){\rm d}\mu({\bf x}-{\bf u}),

where

\pi=\mu\left(\left\{{\bf x}:\|{\bf x}-{\bf u}\|\leqslant\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|\right\}\right)

is a normalizing constant. Then it holds that

\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v},\\
\|{\bf v}\|=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}{\bf u}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v},\\
\|{\bf v}\|=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\rho_{\bf u}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}.

Let us fix

\lambda=\frac{1}{4\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{\|\Sigma\|n}}.

According to Lemma
E.1
, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
(1-\delta)
, we have

\lambda\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v},\\
\|{\bf v}\|=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\rho_{\bf u}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\rho_{\bf u}}\log\mathbb{E}_{{\bf X},\varepsilon}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}\varepsilon}+\frac{{\mathcal{KL}}(\rho_{\bf u},\mu)+\log(1/\delta)}{n}

simultaneously for all
\rho_{\bf u}
, such that
{\bf u}=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}{\bf v}
for some unit vector
{\bf v}
. Applying Lemma
E.2
with
P=\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}
,
Q=1
, and
\beta=2{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})
, we obtain that

{\mathcal{KL}}(\rho_{\bf u},\mu)\leqslant\log 2+{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})\left\|(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top})^{\dagger}{\bf u}\right\|^{2}\leqslant\log 2+{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}).

Besides, since
\|\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}_{1}\varepsilon_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}=\sigma
and

\lambda=\frac{1}{4\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{\|\Sigma\|n}}\leqslant\frac{1}{4\sigma\sqrt{\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sigma\|\xi\|},

Lemma 2 from
Zhivotovskiy [2024]
yields that

\log\mathbb{E}_{{\bf X},\varepsilon}e^{\lambda\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}{\bf X}\varepsilon}\leqslant 4\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|^{2}\leqslant 8\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|^{2}\right)\leqslant 16\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|^{2}.

Here we used the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|^{2}\leqslant 2\|{\bf u}\|^{2}+2\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|^{2}\leqslant 4\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\|^{2}
almost surely.
Hence, with probability at least
(1-\delta)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|B({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})\right\|=\frac{1}{n}\left\|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}B{\bf X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 16\lambda\sigma^{2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2}\|+\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{\lambda n}

\displaystyle=8\sigma\left\|B\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{1/2}B^{\top}B\Sigma^{1/2})+\log(2/\delta)}{n}}.

\square

E.3
Auxiliary results

Lemma E.2
.

Let
\beta
be a positive number and let
P\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
and
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times s}
be arbitrary matrices.
Let
\mathsf{p}
be the probability density on
{\rm Im}((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top}))
(with respect to the volume measure
{\mathcal{V}}
) of the Gaussian distribution
{\mathcal{N}}\big(0,\beta^{-1}\,(QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})\big)
, where
\otimes
stands for the Kronecker product. That is,

\mathsf{p}({\bf x})\propto\exp\left\{-\frac{\beta}{2}{\bf x}^{\top}\big((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})\big)^{\dagger}{\bf x}\right\},\quad{\bf x}\in{\rm Im}((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})).

Fix a matrix
U\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
, such that
{\rm\bf vec}(U)\in{\rm Im}((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top}))
, and set

R=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}\,{\rm Tr}(PP^{\top})\,{\rm Tr}(QQ^{\top})}.

Define the density
\mathsf{p}_{U}
as follows:

\mathsf{p}_{U}({\bf x})=\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}\mathsf{p}({\bf x}-{\rm\bf vec}(U))\mathbbm{1}\big(\|{\bf x}-{\rm\bf vec}(U)\|\leqslant R\big),\quad{\bf x}\in{\rm Im}((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})),

where

\pi_{R}=\int\limits_{\|{\bf x}\|\leqslant R}\mathsf{p}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\mathcal{V}}({\bf x})=\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim\mathsf{p}}\big(\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|\leqslant R\big)

is a normalizing constant. Then it holds that

{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\|P^{{\dagger}}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant\log 2+\frac{\beta}{2}\|P^{{\dagger}}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Proof.

Denote
S=(QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})
and
{\bf u}={\rm\bf vec}(U)
for brevity.
By the definition of
{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})

\displaystyle=\int\log\frac{\mathsf{p}_{U}({\bf x})}{\mathsf{p}({\bf x})}\,\mathsf{p}_{U}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\mathcal{V}}({\bf x})

\displaystyle=\int\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}\,\mathsf{p}_{U}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\mathcal{V}}({\bf x})+\frac{\beta}{2}\int\limits_{\|{\bf x}-{\bf u}\|\leqslant R}\left[{\bf x}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf x}-({\bf x}-{\bf u})^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}({\bf x}-{\bf u})\right]{\rm d}{\mathcal{V}}({\bf x})

\displaystyle=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\int\limits_{\|{\bf x}-{\bf u}\|\leqslant R}\left[2{\bf x}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}-{\bf u}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}\right]{\rm d}{\mathcal{V}}({\bf x})

\displaystyle=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\left(2{\bf u}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}-{\bf u}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}\right)=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}{\bf u}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}.

Let us recall the following properties of the Kronecker product:

\displaystyle\big((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})\big)^{{\dagger}}=(QQ^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\otimes(PP^{\top})^{{\dagger}},

\displaystyle\big((QQ^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\otimes(PP^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\big){\rm\bf vec}(U)={\rm\bf vec}\big((PP^{\top})^{{\dagger}}U(QQ^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\big).

Using these two inequalities, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\bf u}^{\top}S^{{\dagger}}{\bf u}

\displaystyle={\rm\bf vec}(U)^{\top}{\rm\bf vec}\big((PP^{\top})^{{\dagger}}U(QQ^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\big)={\rm Tr}\big(U^{\top}(PP^{\top})^{{\dagger}}U(QQ^{\top})^{{\dagger}}\big)

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}\big(U^{\top}(P^{\dagger})^{\top}P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}Q^{\dagger}\big)={\rm Tr}\big(Q^{\dagger}U^{\top}(P^{\dagger})^{\top}P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle=\|P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Hence, it holds that

{\mathcal{KL}}(\rho_{U},\mu)=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\|P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

To prove the second part of the lemma, note that

\pi_{R}=\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|\leqslant R),\quad\text{where $\boldsymbol{\xi}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\beta^{-1}\,(QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top}))$}.

Using Chebyshev’s inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle\pi_{R}

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|^{2}}{R^{2}}=1-\frac{\mathbb{E}{\rm Tr}(\boldsymbol{\xi}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\top})}{R^{2}}=1-\frac{{\rm Tr}\big((QQ^{\top})\otimes(PP^{\top})\big)}{\beta R^{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{{\rm Tr}\big((QQ^{\top})\big){\rm Tr}\big((PP^{\top})\big)}{\beta R^{2}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2},

and thus,

{\mathcal{KL}}(\mathsf{p}_{U},\mathsf{p})=\log\frac{1}{\pi_{R}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\|P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leqslant\log 2+\frac{\beta}{2}\|P^{\dagger}U(Q^{\dagger})^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

∎

Appendix F
Proof of Corollary
2.7

According to Theorem
2.3
and
2.4
, with probability at least
(1-13\delta/2)
, it holds that

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\right\|\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|+\diamondsuit,

(144)

where
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\Sigma{\bf U}-\Sigma(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}-(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}
and
\diamondsuit
defined in (
15
). In the rest of the proof, we show that the norm of
\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\zeta}
does not exceed

4\left(2\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{2C_{X}\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

In order to do so, we use the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma{\bf U}\right\|+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

(145)

and bound the three terms in the right-hand side one by one using Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
. Before we proceed, we formulate an auxiliary result.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
r_{q}(k)
and
k^{*}=k^{*}(\lambda)
be as defined in (
11
) and (
12
), respectively. Then it holds that

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\big(k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})\big)

and

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)\leqslant 4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*}).

The proof of Lemma
F.1
is postponed to Section
F.1
.
It helps us to verify the conditions of Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
. Indeed, taking into account that
r_{4}(k)\leqslant r_{2}(k)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we observe that the condition (
13
) and Lemma
F.1
yield

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)+\log(4/\delta)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\big(k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})\big)+\log(4/\delta)\leqslant n.

Then, according to Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
, with probability at least
(1-\delta)
, we simultaneously have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\left\|\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\,\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4C_{X}\|\Sigma\|^{2}}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda}\,\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

and

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma{\bf U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 8\sigma\left\|\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{2}}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

In view of Lemma
F.1
, we obtain that, on the same event, it holds that

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma){\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|\leqslant 4C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(146)

and

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\Sigma{\bf U}\right\|\leqslant 8\sigma\sqrt{\frac{k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

(147)

It remains to bound the norm of
\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}
to finish the proof. Note that Lemma
F.1
and (
13
) imply that

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)+\log(4/\delta)\leqslant 4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)\leqslant 4n.

Then, due to Theorem
2.5
, with probability at least
(1-\delta/2)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\Sigma{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{X}\left\|\Sigma(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|\,\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(148)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2C_{X}\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}.

Here we used the fact that

\left\|\Sigma(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\right\|=\max\limits_{1\leqslant j\leqslant d}\frac{\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2\lambda}}.

Summing up the inequalities (
145
)–(
148
) and using the union bound, we obtain that, with probability at least
(1-3\delta/2)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\left(2\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2C_{X}\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(149)

Finally, (
144
) and (
149
) yield that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|+4\left(2\sigma+C_{X}\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|\right)\sqrt{\frac{k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2C_{X}\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*})+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}+\diamondsuit

with probability at least
(1-8\delta)
.

\square

F.1
Proof of Lemma
F.1

Due to the definition of the effective rank, we have

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)=\frac{{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2})}{\|\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\|}=\left(\frac{\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{4}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}.

Taking into account that the operator norm of
\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}
is equal to
\|\Sigma\|^{4}/(\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{4}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{4}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}+\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{4}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{\|\Sigma\|^{2}+2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}1+\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{4}}{\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{2\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(k^{*}+r_{4}(k^{*})\right).

Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\max\limits_{1\leqslant k\leqslant d}\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{(\sigma_{k}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}\right)^{-1}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\min\limits_{1\leqslant k\leqslant d}\frac{(\sigma_{k}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}+\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}1+\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}.

Since the definition of
k^{*}
(see (
12
)) yields that

\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}}\leqslant\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}+\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}}=4\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}

and

\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}\leqslant\frac{(\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}{4\lambda^{2}}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}}\leqslant\frac{(2\lambda+2\lambda)^{2}}{4\lambda^{2}}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{k^{*}+1}^{2}}=4r_{2}(k^{*}),

we get the desired bound:

{\mathtt{r}}\left(\Sigma^{2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-2}\right)\leqslant 4k^{*}+4r_{2}(k^{*}).

\square

Appendix G
Properties of the bias
{\bf b}_{\lambda}

In this section, we provide rigorous proofs of some properties of
{\bf b}_{\lambda}=-2\lambda(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
mentioned in Section
2
. Let us introduce auxiliary notation to simplify further derivations. Throughout this section,
\sigma_{1}\geqslant\sigma_{2}\geqslant\dots\geqslant\sigma_{d}
stand for the eigenvalues of
\Sigma
and

\Sigma=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\sigma_{j}{\bf v}_{j}{\bf v}_{j}^{\top}

is its eigenvalue decomposition. For any
k\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, we define
\beta_{k}={\bf v}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}
and the projections of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
onto the linear span of
{\bf v}_{1},\dots,{\bf v}_{k}
and its orthogonal complement, respectively:

\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\leqslant k}^{\circ}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k}\beta_{j}{\bf v}_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\theta}_{>k}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\leqslant k}^{\circ}.

Similarly to the statement of Corollary
2.7
, we denote
