k^{*}=k^{*}(\lambda)=\max\left\{k\in\mathbb{N}:\sigma_{k}^{2}\geqslant 2\lambda\right\}.

The first result relates the squared norms of
\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}
and
\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}
with the ones of
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\leqslant k^{*}}^{\circ}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{>k^{*}}^{\circ}
.

Lemma G.1
.

With the notation introduced above, it holds that

\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}\leqslant\frac{\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}}{4}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\right\|^{2}

and

\frac{\left\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}}{2\lambda}\leqslant\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\right\|^{2}.

Proof.

Let us first elaborate on the upper bound on
\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\|^{2}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\lambda)^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|^{2}=4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}

\displaystyle=4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}+4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}.

Due to the Cauchy inequality, we have
8\lambda\sigma_{j}^{2}\leqslant(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}
for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. This yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}+4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\sigma_{j}^{-1}\beta_{j}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}=\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\right\|^{2}.

Due to the definition of
k^{*}
, the right-hand side does not exceed
\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\|^{2}/4+\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\|^{2}
. Similarly, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}}{2\lambda}

\displaystyle=2\lambda\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{3}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}+2\lambda\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{3}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}\leqslant 2\lambda\sum\limits_{j=1}^{k^{*}}\sigma_{j}^{-1}\beta_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{j>k^{*}}\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\lambda\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\right\|^{2}\leqslant\sigma_{k^{*}}^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{\leqslant k^{*}}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}_{>k^{*}}\right\|^{2}.

∎

Lemma G.2
.

Let
\tau^{2}=2\lambda
. Then it holds that

\max\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2},\frac{\left\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}}{2\lambda}\right\}\leqslant 2\tau^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma+\tau I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|^{2}.

Proof.

The proof is trivial. First, note that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\lambda)^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{2}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|^{2}=4\lambda^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{4}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{2}}\leqslant\tau^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\tau^{2}}\leqslant 2\tau^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}+\tau)^{2}}.

The expression in the right-hand side is nothing but
2\tau^{2}\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma+\tau I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}
. This concludes the first part of the proof. The second one follows from the inequalities

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\Sigma^{3/2}{\bf b}_{\lambda}\right\|^{2}}{2\lambda}

\displaystyle=2\lambda\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{3}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+2\lambda)^{2}}=\tau^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{3}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tau^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\tau^{2}}\leqslant 2\tau^{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{j}\beta_{j}^{2}}{(\sigma_{j}+\tau)^{2}}=2\tau^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma+\tau I_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\right\|^{2}.

∎

Appendix H
Proof of Lemma
3.1
and auxiliary results

H.1
Proof of Lemma
3.1

We start the proof with the following non-probabilistic lemma.

Lemma H.1
.

For some
\rho_{0}\leqslant 1/8
, define sets

\displaystyle\Theta

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\;\text{and}\;\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant 5\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/96\right\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid\|\boldsymbol{\eta}-{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\left\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\mid\|A\|\leqslant 3\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3\right\}.

Suppose that

(i)

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu/7
and
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/(18\cdot 16)
;

(ii)

\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\lambda/(2\cdot 96)
and
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|
;

(iii)

\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\lambda/(2\cdot 96)
and

\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/(2\cdot 96)
.

Then, we have the following:

\displaystyle({\bf 0},{\bf Z},\widehat{\Sigma}),(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},A^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}),(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{A},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\in\Theta\times\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{H}.

and
\Theta\times\mathsf{H}\times\mathsf{A}\subset\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
. Moreover,
\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
.

Next, we will choose
\lambda
, to satisfy conditions
(ii)
-
(iii)
using the following proposition proved in Section
H.4
.

Proposition H.2
.

Fix
\delta\in(0;1)
and
\rho_{0}\leqslant 1/8
. Grant Assumptions
2.1
and
2.2
. Assume that

(i)

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu/7
;

(ii)

\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
;

(iii)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|}\wedge\sqrt{\lambda}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2^{11}\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\rho_{0}\mu}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2^{13}\|\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\rho_{0}\mu}(1+C_{X})\sqrt{\frac{4{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)}{n}},

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\geqslant 2^{12}(1+C_{X})^{2}\left({\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)\right).

Then, conditions
(i)
-
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
are satisfied and the upper bound

\displaystyle\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Clearly, Lemma
H.1
combined with Proposition
H.2
implies the desired result.

H.2
Proof of Lemma
H.1

Step 1. Bounding the norm of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
.
We start from the following chain of inequalities:

\displaystyle\lambda/2\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|^{2}\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{\Sigma})\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}({\bf 0},{\bf Z},\widehat{\Sigma})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bf Z}\|^{2}.

It implies
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|^{2}\leqslant\|{\bf Z}\|^{2}/\lambda
. Next, we have

\displaystyle\|{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|{\bf U}\|

Using
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|
from condition
(ii)
,
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/(18\cdot 8)
from condition
(i)
and
\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/18
from condition
(iii)
, we infer

\displaystyle\|{\bf Z}\|\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/6.

(150)

It yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\|{\bf Z}\|/\sqrt{\lambda}\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu/6.

(151)

Note that bound (
150
) also implies that
({\bf 0},{\bf Z},\widehat{\Sigma})\in\Theta\times\mathsf{H}\times\mathsf{A}
.

Step 2. Weak perturbation bounds.
A bound on
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|
allows us applying the following lemma:

Lemma H.3
.

Assume that
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant 4\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
. Then, if
\max\{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\}\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu
for
\rho_{0}\leqslant 1/2
, then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\frac{8\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Applying Lemma
B.2
with
\rho_{0}
in place of
\rho
, we get
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
, and the latter is at most
\rho_{0}\mu/7
by the conditions of the lemma to be proved. Therefore, assumptions of Lemma
H.3
are satisfied, and its conclusion holds.

Step 3. Proving that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta
.
Since
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu
due to Lemma
B.2
(iv)
, the vector
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
belongs to
\Theta
obviously. Next, we check that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
. It requires to bound

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\frac{8\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

(152)

Using conditions
(ii)
,
(iii)
, we bound the latter by
4\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/96+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{96}=5\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/96
, so
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
.

Step 4. Proving that
\widehat{A},A^{*}\in\mathsf{A}
.
Using

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|^{2}\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}({\bf 0},{\bf Z},\widehat{\Sigma})=\|{\bf Z}\|^{2}/2,

we get
\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\|{\bf Z}\|/\mu
. Using (
150
), we establish
\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}/6\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
. This implies

\displaystyle\|\widehat{A}\|\leqslant 3\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3,

since
\|\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|
due to condition
(ii)
. Thus, we have
\widehat{A}\in\mathsf{A}
.

Next, applying Lemma
B.2
(i)
, we get with
\rho_{0}
in place of
\rho
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|A^{*}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\sqrt{\lambda}/7+\|\Sigma\|\leqslant\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3,

where we used conditions
(i)
,
(ii)
in the second inequality. Hence,
\|A^{*}\|\leqslant 3\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3
and
A^{*}\in\mathsf{A}
.

Step 5. Proving that
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\mathsf{H}
.
To prove that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\mathsf{H}
, note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z})
. It yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/2+\|{\bf Z}\|/2

\displaystyle\leqslant(3\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3)\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/2+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/12

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|/2+\sqrt{\lambda}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/6+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/12

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|/2+3\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|/2+\sqrt{\lambda}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/6+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/12

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{6\cdot 16}+\frac{15\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{96}+\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{6}+\frac{\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}{12}\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3.

where we used
\widehat{A}\in\mathsf{A}
and
\|{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/6
from (
150
) for the second inequality,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
from Lemma
B.2
(iv)
for the fourth inequality,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
and
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\leqslant\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/(18\cdot 16)
from condition
(i)
for the fifth inequality.

To prove
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\in\mathsf{H}
, we bound it as follows:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}-{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{2}(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\Sigma\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ})-{\bf Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{\|A^{*}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{2}\right)+\|{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|A^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\|}{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\sqrt{\lambda}/3+\|{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/(18\cdot 16)+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/21+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/6\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3

where we used
A^{*}\in\mathsf{A}
in the second inequality. To establish the fourth inequality, we applied bound (
150
) and condition
(i)
.

Step 6. Endgame.
We are left to prove that
\Theta\times\mathsf{H}\times\mathsf{A}\subset\Upsilon(\rho_{0})
. We state it as the following proposition.

Proposition H.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\Theta\times\mathsf{H}\times\mathsf{A}\subset\Upsilon(\rho_{0}).

Proof.

Consider arbitrary
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)\in\Theta\times\mathsf{H}\times\mathsf{A}
. It is enough to prove that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu
and
\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
. The former inequality is satisfied by the definition of
\Theta
. To satisfy the second inequality, we bound
\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|
as follows:

\displaystyle\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\|{\bf Z}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\eta}-{\bf Z}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant(\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|+\|{\bf Z}\|+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\sqrt{\lambda}/3+\|{\bf Z}\|+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+5\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/96+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3+\|{\bf Z}\|+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}.

where the second inequality holds by the definitions of
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{H}
, and the fourth inequality holds by the definition of
\Theta
. Due to (
150
), we have
\|{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/6
, hence, we have

\displaystyle\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\leqslant\left(1-11/96\right)\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}+\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|.

Due to Lemma
B.2
(iv)
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
. Finally, the quantity
\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
is at most
11\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/96
due to condition
(i)
, so
\|A\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}
.
∎

H.3
Proof of Lemma
H.3

Step 1. Constructing a finite difference equation.
The idea is to employ the equation

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})={\bf 0},

which holds by definitions of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\upsilon}}=(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})
and
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{*}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})
. We have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nabla}{\mathtt{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)=\begin{pmatrix}-A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}+A^{\top}A\boldsymbol{\theta}+\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}\\
-{\bf Z}+2\boldsymbol{\eta}-A\boldsymbol{\theta}\\
A\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}+\mu^{2}(A-\widehat{\Sigma})\end{pmatrix}\text{ and }\nabla{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)=\begin{pmatrix}-A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}+A^{\top}A\boldsymbol{\theta}+\lambda\boldsymbol{\theta}\\
-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}+2\boldsymbol{\eta}-A\boldsymbol{\theta}\\
A\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}+\mu^{2}(A-\Sigma)\end{pmatrix}.

Define an operator
{\Delta}
, acting on a matrix-valued functions
f(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
as
{\Delta}(f)=f(\widehat{A},\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\theta})-f(A^{*},\eta^{*},\theta^{*})
. Note that it possesses the following identities for any two matrix-valued functions
f,g
such that their product is defined:

\displaystyle{\Delta}(fg)={\Delta}(f)\,g(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})+f(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})\,{\Delta}(g);

\displaystyle{\Delta}(fg)=f(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})\,{\Delta}(g)+{\Delta}(f)\,g(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*}).

(153)

Using
\nabla{\mathtt{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})-\nabla{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},A^{*})=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-{\Delta}(A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta})+{\Delta}(A^{\top}A\boldsymbol{\theta})+\lambda{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})&={\bf 0}\\
-({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z})+2{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta})-{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})&={\bf 0}\\
{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})-{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})+\mu^{2}{\Delta}(A)&=\mu^{2}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\end{cases}

The second equation implies
{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{1}{2}({\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})+({\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}))
. Substituting it in the above system, we get

\displaystyle{\Delta}(A^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta})+{\Delta}^{\top}(A)\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}+{\Delta}^{\top}(A)\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*},

\displaystyle{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})

\displaystyle={\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}{\Delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}{\Delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

where we denote
{\bf Z}-\mathbb{E}{\bf Z}
by
{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}
for brevity.
Since
{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})={\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}{\Delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
and
{\Delta}(A^{\top}A\boldsymbol{\theta})=\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})+{\Delta}^{\top}(A)A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
due to (
153
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}+{\Delta}^{\top}(A)(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})+\lambda{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})&={\bf 0},\\
\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}-\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}+(A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}){\Delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\mu^{2}{\Delta}(A)&=\mu^{2}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma).\end{cases}

(154)

Step 2. Bounding the norm of
\|{\Delta}(A)\|
.
Deriving
{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})={\Delta}(A)\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+A^{*}{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
from (
153
) and substituting it into the last equation of (
154
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\Delta}(A)\left(\mu^{2}I_{d}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\theta}\widehat{\theta}^{\top}\right)=\mu^{2}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)-(A^{*}\theta^{*}-\eta^{*}){\Delta}^{\top}(\theta)+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\widehat{\theta}^{\top}.

For brevity, denote
A^{*}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}
by
{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
.
Rearranging terms, we get

\displaystyle{\Delta}(A)

\displaystyle=\mu^{2}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\left(\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\right)^{-1}-{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\Delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\left(\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top}\left(\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\right)^{-1},

and, therefore, we bound

\displaystyle\|{\Delta}(A)\|\leqslant\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\mu^{-2}\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|+\frac{1}{2}\mu^{-2}\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|.

(155)

Step 3. Establishing a bound on
\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|
.
We bound
\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|
using
\|{\Delta}(A)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|
and
\|{\Delta}^{\top}(A){\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|
. From the first equation of (
154
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}+{\Delta}^{\top}(A){\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\lambda{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\bf 0}.

Since
{\Delta}(A\boldsymbol{\theta})=\widehat{A}{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+{\Delta}(A)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right){\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}-{\Delta}^{\top}(A){\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}{\Delta}(A)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}.

(156)

Next, we bound the norms
\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|
and
\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\widehat{A}\right\|
. While the first norm is clearly at most
1/\lambda
, for the second norm we need to maximize

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\widehat{A}\right\|=\max_{i}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}/2+\lambda}

over all possible singular values
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}
of
\widehat{A}
. However, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}/2+\lambda}\leqslant\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{x}{x^{2}/2+\lambda}=\frac{\sqrt{2\lambda}}{2\lambda}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}

by elementary calculus.
Thus, multiplying both sides of (
156
) by
\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}
and applying bounds on
\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\right\|
and
\left\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}^{\top}\widehat{A}+\lambda I_{d}\right)^{-1}\widehat{A}\right\|
, we derive

\displaystyle\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|}{\sqrt{8\lambda}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\|{\Delta}^{\top}(A){\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}\|{\Delta}(A)\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|}{\sqrt{8\lambda}}+\frac{\|{\Delta}(A)\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|}{\sqrt{8\lambda}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|}{\mu^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\frac{\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right),

where the third inequality holds due to (
155
).
According to Lemma
B.2
(iii)
, we have
\|{\bf b}_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
. It implies

\displaystyle\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}}{\sqrt{8\lambda}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{-2}\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right).

By assumptions of the lemma, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|
and
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|
are at most
\rho_{0}\mu
, which implies

\displaystyle\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|}{\sqrt{8\lambda}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\rho_{0}\mu^{-1}/2\|{\Delta}_{\bf Z}\|\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\rho^{2}_{0}\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|.

Since
{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}=(\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+{\bf U}
, we may simplify the inequality above and obtain

\displaystyle(1-2\rho^{2}_{0})\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|{\Delta}_{{\bf Z}}\|}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot(1+2\rho^{2}_{0})+\frac{\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)+\frac{\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

where we used
\rho_{0}\leqslant 1/2
.
It implies the desired bound on
{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\|{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\leqslant\frac{4\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Applying bound
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
from Lemma
B.2
(iv)
, we obtain the lemma.
\square

H.4
Proof of Proposition
H.2

Step 1. Bound on
\|{\bf U}\|
.
To ensure conditions
(ii)
,
(iii)
, we shall obtain bounds on
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|
and
\|{\bf U}\|
with high probability. For the latter quantity, we apply Theorem
2.6
with
B=I_{d}
, and obtain

\displaystyle\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}},

(157)

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, provided
n\geqslant{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)
.

Step 2. Bound on
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|
.
First, we will show that under Assumption
2.1
, a random vector
{\bf X}_{1}
satisfies
\psi_{2}-L_{2}
-equivalence. In what follows,
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}},\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}}
stand for
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
Orlicz norms.

Lemma H.5

(Lemma B.3 of
Puchkin and Rakhuba [2024]
)
.

Suppose that a random vector
{\bf X}
satisfies Assumption
2.1
. Then, for any vector
{\bf u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\bf u}^{\top}{\bf X}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leqslant\frac{1+C_{X}}{\log 2}\cdot{\bf u}^{\top}\Sigma{\bf u}.

Then, we deduce the concentration bound on
\widehat{\Sigma}
from the following theorem.

Lemma H.6

(Theorem 1 of
Zhivotovskiy [2024]
)
.

Assume that
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
are independent copies of a
d
by
d
positive semi-definite symmetric matrix
M
with mean
\mathbb{E}M=\Sigma
. Let
M
satisfy for some
\kappa\geqslant 1

\displaystyle\|{\bf u}^{\top}M{\bf u}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leqslant\kappa^{2}{\bf u}^{\top}\Sigma{\bf u}

for all
{\bf u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then, for any
\delta>0
, with probability
1-\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{i}-\Sigma\right\|\leqslant 20\kappa^{2}\|\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{4{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(1/\delta)}{n}},

whenever
n\geqslant 4{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(1/\delta)

Combining the above two lemmata and using an inequality
\|\xi^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leqslant\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}
suitable for any random variable
\xi
, we derive

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\frac{20(1+C_{X})}{\log 2}\cdot\|\Sigma\|\sqrt{\frac{4{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

(158)

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
.

Step 3. Satisfiability of conditions
(ii)
,
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
.
Note that if

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2^{13}\|\Sigma\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{\rho_{0}\mu}(1+C_{X})\sqrt{\frac{4{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)}{n}},

(159)

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\geqslant 2^{12}(1+C_{X})^{2}\left({\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)\right),

(160)

then condition
(ii)
of Lemma
H.1
is satisfied with probability at least
1-\delta/2
due to (
158
). Similarly, due to (
157
), condition
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
is satisfied with probability at least
1-\delta/2
if

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\|\Sigma\|}\wedge\sqrt{\lambda}\geqslant\frac{2^{11}\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}}{\rho_{0}\mu}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(161)

holds. The union bound implies that conditions
(ii)
-
(iii)
simultaniously hold with probability at least
1-\delta
, provided inequalities (
159
)-(
161
) are satisfied.

\square

Appendix I
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Step 1. Establishing a finite difference equation.
We consider the following three stationary point conditions:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t},(A_{t-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}+{\bf Z})/2,A_{t-1})

\displaystyle={\bf 0},

\displaystyle\nabla_{A}{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t},(A_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}+{\bf Z})/2,A_{t})

\displaystyle={\bf 0},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+{\bf Z})/2,\widehat{A})

\displaystyle={\bf 0},

The first two equations can be expanded as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left(\frac{1}{2}A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+\lambda I_{d}\right)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}A^{\top}_{t-1}{\bf Z}={\bf 0}\\
A_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}^{\top}/2-\frac{1}{2}{\bf Z}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}+\mu^{2}(A_{t}-\widehat{\Sigma})={\bf 0}.\end{cases}

(162)

For a matrix-valued function
f(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\eta},A)
, we define an operator

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(f)=f(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t},A_{t})-f(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A}).

Note that for product of any matrix-valued functions
f,g
of appropriate shapes, we can write

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(fg)

\displaystyle=f(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t},A_{t})\Delta_{t}(g)+\Delta_{t}(f)g(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(fg)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{t}(f)g(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t},A_{t})+f(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})\Delta_{t}(g).

(163)

Using this notation, we subtract
\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+{\bf Z})/2,\widehat{A})={\bf 0}
from the first equation of (
162
) and
\nabla_{A}{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+{\bf Z})/2,\widehat{A})={\bf 0}
from the second equation of (
162
), and obtain

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left(\frac{1}{2}A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+\lambda I_{d}\right)\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{t-1}(A^{\top}A)\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{t-1}^{\top}(A){\bf Z}&={\bf 0},\\
\Delta_{t}(A)(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}^{\top}/2+\mu^{2}I_{d})+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{A}\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})-\frac{1}{2}{\bf Z}\Delta^{\top}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})&={\bf 0}.\end{cases}

Expanding
\Delta_{t-1}(A^{\top}A)=A_{t-1}^{\top}\Delta_{t-1}(A)+\Delta_{t-1}^{\top}(A)\widehat{A}
,
\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})=\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}^{\top}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\Delta_{t}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
due to (
163
), and rearranging terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})&=-\frac{1}{2}(A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}/2+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\left[A_{t-1}^{\top}\Delta_{t-1}(A)\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{t-1}(A)(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z})\right],\\
\Delta_{t}(A)&=-\frac{1}{2}\left[\widehat{A}\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}^{\top}+(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z})\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right](\mu^{2}I_{d}+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}^{\top}/2)^{-1}.\end{cases}

(164)

Step 2. Recursively bounding the norm of
\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
and
\Delta_{t}(A)
.
Let us start with the first equation of the system above. We have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\leqslant\|\Delta_{t-1}(A)\|\left[\|(A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}A_{t-1}^{\top}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|(A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\right].

Let
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{d}
be the singular values of
A_{t-1}
. Using elementary calculus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+\lambda I_{d})^{-1}A_{t-1}^{\top}\|=\max_{i}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}+\lambda}\leqslant\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{x}{x^{2}+\lambda}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Then, using
\|(A_{t-1}^{\top}A_{t-1}+2\lambda I_{d})^{-1}\|\leqslant 1/(2\lambda)
, we elaborate

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\leqslant\|\Delta_{t-1}(A)\|\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right].

(165)

Next, we analyze the norm of
\Delta_{t}(A)
. From the second equation (
164
), we infer

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|\leqslant\mu^{-2}\left[\|\widehat{A}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}\|+\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\right]\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|/2.

Bounding
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{t}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|
and applying bound (
165
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\left[\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\right]\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]\|\Delta_{t-1}(A)\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\|\widehat{A}\|\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]^{2}\|\Delta_{t-1}(A)\|^{2}.

Step 3. Exploiting the recursion.
We will prove by induction, that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|\leqslant\rho_{0}^{2t}\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|.

(166)

Clearly, the base
t=0
holds, by the definition of
A_{0}=\widehat{\Sigma}
. Assume that (
166
) holds for
t
, and let us prove it for
t+1
. We have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\left[\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|+\|\widehat{A}\|\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\left[\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|+\|\widehat{A}\|\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]\cdot\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|.

Next, we use the fact that
\widehat{A}\in\mathsf{A}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
. The definitions of sets
\Theta,\mathsf{A}
imply
\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
,
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\mu
and

\displaystyle\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\|\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/3+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3\leqslant 2\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3.

(167)

It yields

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mu^{-2}}{2}\left[\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|+\rho_{0}\mu\sqrt{\lambda}/3+\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|/3+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{3\sqrt{\lambda}}\right]\cdot\frac{2\rho_{0}\mu}{3\sqrt{\lambda}}\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}\cdot\left[\rho_{0}/3+\frac{4\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{9\mu\lambda}\right]\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|.

Bound (
167
) does not allow establishing
\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|\leqslant\rho_{0}^{2}\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|
from the above inequality, which would imply (
166
). Instead, we use the definition of
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})\leqslant{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ},(\widehat{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+{\bf Z})/2,\widehat{\Sigma})=\frac{1}{4}\|\widehat{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf Z}\|^{2}+\lambda/2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|^{2}.

Using
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}=(\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+{\bf Z})/2
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{A})=\frac{1}{4}\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|^{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|,

so we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}-{\bf Z}\|+\sqrt{2\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|=\|{\bf U}\|+\sqrt{2\lambda}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|,

(168)

where we used decomposition
{\bf Z}=\widehat{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}+{\bf U}
. Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\cdot\left[\rho_{0}/3+\frac{4\|\widehat{A}\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\|\|{\bf U}\|}{9\mu\lambda}+\frac{2\|\widehat{A}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right]\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|.

Under assumptions of the theorem, Proposition
H.2
implies the satisfaction of conditions
(i)
-
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
and the upper bound

\displaystyle\|{\bf U}\|\leqslant 8\sigma\|\Sigma\|^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathtt{r}}(\Sigma)+\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

(169)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. So, we assume that these conditions hold.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{A}\|\leqslant\|\widehat{\Sigma}\|+\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant 2\|\Sigma\|+\sqrt{\lambda}/3,

where we used condition
(ii)
of Lemma
H.1
and the fact that
\widehat{A}\in\mathsf{A}
. It yields

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\cdot\left[\rho_{0}/3+\frac{8\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{4\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{27\mu}+\frac{2\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf U}\|}{9\mu\lambda}+\frac{\|{\bf U}\|}{27\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right]\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|.

Using the fact that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Theta
and conditions
(ii)
,
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|\leqslant\rho_{0}\cdot\left[\rho_{0}/2+\frac{8\|\Sigma\|\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{9\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}\right]\cdot\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|

From Lemma
H.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\frac{4\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|)}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{7}+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

where we used
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/7
from condition
(ii)
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|
from Lemma
B.2
(iv)
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\leqslant\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|+\frac{8\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{7}+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\leqslant\frac{15\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{7}+\frac{2\|{\bf U}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

(170)

and

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t+1}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho_{0}\cdot\left[\rho_{0}/2+\frac{120\|\Sigma\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|}{63\mu\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{16\|\Sigma\|\|{\bf U}\|}{9\mu\lambda}\right]\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho_{0}^{2}\,\|\Delta_{t}(A)\|,

where we used conditions
(i)
,
(iii)
of Lemma
H.1
. Hence, the inequality (
166
) indeed holds.

Step 4. Final bound.
Substituting (
166
) into (
165
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\leqslant\rho_{0}^{2(t-1)}\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\cdot\left[\frac{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\frac{\|\widehat{A}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\bf Z}\|}{2\lambda}\right]

Using bounds (
168
),(
170
) and
\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\lambda}/3
from the fact that
\widehat{A}\in\mathsf{A}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\|\leqslant\rho_{0}^{2(t-1)}\left[2\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\circ}\|+\frac{\|{\bf U}\|}{3\sqrt{\lambda}}\right].

Replacing
t
in the inequality above with
T
and bounding
\|{\bf U}\|
by (
169
) complete the proof.

\square
